# Partsmith M1 gap work list (after the reference stability phases)

This list merges the 43 confirmed gaps and the 11 critic additions. Duplicates are combined (see "Merged inputs" at the end). Two things are left out: the reference stability plan (phases 1 to 4) and ui.md open issue 17.

It was produced on 2026-10-13 by comparing the technical design and every spec with the code, area by area, with each gap checked by a reviewer who tried to refute it. Items R-1 and R-2 are already folded into section 10 of [reference-stability-and-errors.md](reference-stability-and-errors.md). The items that wait for the repository owner are named in the status paragraph below.

**Status at the end of M1 (2026-10-31, updated on 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05).** Waves D and E are on `main`: ENG-7 and EX-11, then SK-2's property tests, B-2's in-app notice and ENG-8. On 2026-11-04 the owner decided the human gates this list held open, all but two parts: SK-2's review of the nightly workflow (its schedule, runner time and skip rule, delivery.md 8) and whether the root AGENTS.md's em dash rule names way 1's exception (ADR 0033, B-2). The ADR log records each decision as `Accepted (owner, 2026-11-04)` with the option chosen. Each item's status line of that date says what it settles. Wave F builds what the decisions ask for, one track at a time:

- records: the decisions in the ADRs and their log, the specs' review markers, the root AGENTS.md (the license invariant of ADR 0034 and the `pnpm -C ui notices` line, with the owner's leave), README.md's address `https://partsmith.pages.dev` and this list.
- appimage: the AppImage leaves M1 (ADR 0034 choice 1): `bundle.targets`, the release matrix and the dry run, README.md, desktop.md, delivery.md 3.6 and test 13, and the release checklist's AppImage item and the install notes in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`, with the owner's leave. ADR 0034's amendment of 2026-11-04 records it (B-1, B-13).
- web-source: `occt-7.9.3-source.tar.gz` written into every Pages deployment (delivery.md 2.13 and open issue 15, B-2).
- windows-crt: OCCT and the Rust crates link the Visual C++ runtime (`libcmt`, `libvcruntime`) and the C++ standard library (`libcpmt`) statically on Windows. The app and the desktop tests take the Universal C Runtime from Windows. `rust-platforms` and the Windows release bundle check the app's imports (ADR 0038 option (i), B-8).
- bench-gate: a timed `bench50` run from an empty cache on the macOS arm64 runner of `platforms.yml` judges technical design section 14's full-rebuild gate. A result over 1 s goes to the owner with an ADR that raises the budget or schedules speed work.
- reattach: a host's re-attach by pick is not offered when no stored frame keeps the sketch's motion, the rule of engine.md section 3.22 (ADR 0023, engine.md open issue 16, ui.md open issue 25). Spec of 2026-11-04: the UI cannot see the motions the rule compares. It asks the engine for the plane to store with a new command, `attach_plane` (engine.md 3.22, protocol.md command 35). It refuses a pick the engine gives no plane for with the hint `attach_moved` (ui.md 3.16.1 and 4.3). Tests: engine 166 and 167, protocol 88 (34 and 87 updated), U-146 (updated), U-250, E-108 and E-165 with the fixture `reattach-moved.ptsm`. The command changes the protocol schema: ADR 0055 records it as proposed, needs human review, with the options the owner can still pick (ADR 0016's entry of 2026-11-04). engine.md open issue 16 and ui.md open issue 25 are resolved, with re-basing later features recorded as declined by the owner. The review of 2026-11-04 added a comparison of motions within 1e-9 (a datum's motion can differ from its face's by rounding), the UI hint `attach_datum_unbuilt`, ADR 0055's open choice for unknown motions and engine.md open issue 23 (a new base of a datum plane), the last two proposed, needs human review. Built on 2026-11-04: `Session::attach_plane` and command 35 in the engine and the protocol, with `Attach`, `TurnDrawingBack` and drag commits comparing motions the same way, and in the UI `CoreApi.attachPlane` and the hints `attach_moved` and `attach_datum_unbuilt`. The pick asks the core and commits the plane it returns in one task of the mutation queue. The protocol schema and the UI's generated types are regenerated. Review fixes of 2026-11-04: a sketch at or after the rollback marker, or after the focus feature, is read in a recompute without them (it read as without motion and took any plane, test 166 (h)); a sketch without motion stands where the descriptor showed a face that moves only after it, the same with the marker before or after that move (test 166 (f)); the UI checks `attach_after` and `attach_datum_unbuilt` in the task that asks the core and sees a suppress queued just before the pick (U-250 (i)); `attach_moved` reads "the two do not move together" and "could shift", and names no class of features. ADR 0055 gains open choice 2, how a pick reads "not offered" (refused when picked, built, or the origin plane buttons dimmed), proposed, needs human review. The web core is rebuilt.
- python-links: Python's save and exports write through a linked document file (ADR 0037 option (A), python-api.md 3.1, 3.7 and open issue 10, PY-1's follow-up). Spec of 2026-11-04: python-api.md 3.1, 3.7 and section 4, tests 118 to 122, open issue 10 resolved, file-format.md 3.11. A link that leads to no file fails the write, as on the desktop (desktop.md test 72). The CLI's `export --output` and `schema --write` onto such a link replace the link: ADR 0037's first amendment of 2026-11-04 records it, proposed, needs human review (cli.md open issue 12). Built on 2026-11-04: `write_through_link` in `python/partsmith/_files.py`. `Document.save` uses it for the document, and the export functions for every file they write. Assets keep `write_atomic`. The review of 2026-11-04 added the operating system's look through the link (`os.stat` first, under Linux's `fs.protected_symlinks`, and `ESTALE` when `realpath` names another file) and kept permissions (test 123). Python's replacement of a read-only file is proposed, needs human review (python-api.md open issue 12, ADR 0037's second amendment). The desktop and the CLI resolve links the same unchecked way (desktop.md open issue 14, cli.md open issue 13).
- step-roundtrip: runner test 5 imports each STEP file it exports and checks its volume (ADR 0046 option (c), delivery.md open issue 18, EX-6). Spec of 2026-11-04: delivery.md 2.8 (STEP round trip), section 4's two runner rows, test 107 and open issue 18 resolved; ADR 0046's amendment of 2026-11-04. The review of 2026-11-04 had each worker import its STEP file right after its export (the STEP reads take turns under the kernel's mutex), gave the check its expected volume and import as arguments (test 69 stands in for the import) and added test 107's case of the binary's file against another expected volume. Built on 2026-11-04: `imported_volume`, `expected_volume` and the round trip in `check_export` (`tests/examples/export.rs`), and `each_in_parallel` with a headless session per worker in `examples.rs`. Review fixes of 2026-11-04: runner test 5 and test 107's two cases of the binary's file share `export_and_check`, `Example::builds` is gone, and the times are in delivery.md open issue 4 and ADR 0046's amendment alone.
- notices-wrap: the notices generator wraps the paragraphs Partsmith writes itself at about 80 columns, and license texts stay as their packages ship them (ui.md open issue 44 option (iii), B-2).
- strict-source: later on 2026-11-04 the owner chose to ask no lawyer and to take the strictest reading of LGPL 2.1 sections 4 and 6 for ADR 0034's four questions. Every release and every web deployment carries vendored archives of the Rust crates and npm packages it is built from. `deploy` requires them. The desktop notices list the code the NSIS installer adds (delivery.md 2.9 and 2.13, B-2).

No wave F or wave G track moves the jobs of `platforms.yml` into `ci.yml` under the promotion rule the owner accepted (delivery.md 2.14, B-8 and B-9). On 2026-11-05 the owner decided that the move follows ADR 0038's rule once the jobs pass two platforms runs after wave F. Run 14 (41926898084, on 0ae7337) is the first, and the first with `full-rebuild`. Run 15 (42156838426, on a3df7f5) is the second. On 2026-11-06 both had passed every job and every row with no rerun. `full-rebuild` is never promoted (ADR 0054). The move leaves checklist item 2 as it is in both copies. In `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`, the owner's file, it changes only the sentences that name a moved job. A job that makes CI's wall time longer than its budget (delivery.md 5) moves only with a budget change. A budget change follows the owner's rule of 2026-11-05 (ADR 0057's method). Wave H's promotion track moves them (B-8 and B-9, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06).

**Decisions of 2026-11-05.** The owner decided every gate left open after wave F. The owner also set a standing rule for every choice after them: the most open-source approach, the safest approach, the industry standard, and the recommendation of the track that meets the choice. The ADR log records a decision made by name as `Accepted (owner, 2026-11-05)` with the option chosen, and a choice the rule settles as `Accepted (owner's rule of 2026-11-05)` with the options considered (docs/adr/README.md). Each item's status line of 2026-11-05 says what it settles. Settled as built, with nothing left to build: the pinned build tools and actions (delivery.md 8); `attach_plane` with ADR 0055's option (a), ADR 0016's entry of 2026-11-04, and open choices 1 (ii) and 2 (i); the desktop security review (the content security policy, the frozen prototype, `MAX_GRANTS` 100 kept across restarts and `assets/` writes next to granted documents, desktop.md open issue 5 closed); the spec open issues signed off as built (kernel.md 8 and 9, features.md 20, recompute.md 7, cli.md 8 and 9, ui.md 20, io.md 4); the nightly workflow, daily at 04:23 UTC with its skip rule; the examples rules (the 1e-6 tolerance, a bless only with `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1`, assets never rewritten); the Cloudflare project on the owner's account; and the desktop's provisional icon, its 1440 by 900 window (minimum 960 by 600) and its close guard wording. Wave G builds the rest, one track at a time:

- records: the decisions and the standing rule in the ADRs, the specs and this list. Done on 2026-11-05. Its review put each part the owner decided by name in the log's form, `Accepted (owner, 2026-11-05)` with the option chosen (ADRs 0016, 0023, 0033, 0034, 0037, 0038, 0054 and 0055). The CLI's read-only refusal names every file the CLI replaces: the document `fmt` rewrites, export files and schema files (ADR 0037, cli.md open issue 12). ADR 0054 decision 6 says the ADR for the first median carries the owner's status.
- archive-contents: lightningcss's source at each locked version, with the MPL-2.0 crates it compiles in, in the npm archives; `licenseAllowed` over every npm package of the archives; MIT-0, Python-2.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0 in `deny.toml`'s allow list with an ADR row; `libdbus-sys`'s `vendor/dbus/` out of the crate archive; and `tauri-cli` 2.12.2's own locked crates in the desktop archive (delivery.md 8, B-2, DEL-2).
- source-artifacts: OCCT's source archive, the crate and npm source archives of the artifact's scope and Partsmith's own source archive with every CI, platforms and release artifact that carries OCCT's compiled code, and `/partsmith-source.tar.gz` in every deployment (delivery.md 8 and open issue 15, B-2).
- windows-runtime: zlib compression in the Windows installer, a Windows-only notices entry for the Visual C++ runtime, and Windows' own Universal C Runtime for every Windows program with the Visual C++ runtime kept static (delivery.md 8, ADR 0038, B-2 and B-8).
- datum-base: a new base of a datum plane that changes its motion while later features build on its sketches is refused, as `attach_plane` refuses a re-attach (engine.md open issue 23, option (a)). Spec of 2026-11-05: engine.md section 3.22 (*A datum plane's new base*: the datum's sketches, at any depth of datums on it, used by another feature; the motion of its old base, the base it had when the open transaction began, known; the new base's motion unknown or different; one helper builds the datum with a base alone to get either motion), 3.21's reverts, tests 168 to 170, and open issue 23 resolved. `attach_plane` answers for a datum plane too (protocol.md command 35). The protocol schema changes in descriptions only (ADR 0016's entry of 2026-11-05). ui.md 2.6, 3.7 and 4.3: the inspector's Base plane field asks the core before a pick lands and refuses a pick it gets no plane for with the hint `datum_base_moved` (or `attach_after` and `attach_datum_unbuilt`), refuses the edited datum itself with `datum_base_self` without asking, and a Restore asks for its saved base first. U-251, E-166 with the fixture `datum-base-lifted.ptsm`, and E-108's row for it. features/datum_plane.md section 3.2 names the rule. ADR 0055's amendment of 2026-11-05 records eight choices of the owner's rule: the existing command, any base for a datum whose old base gives no motion, a refused base whose motion is unknown, a revert to a refused base left out, `TurnDrawingBack` and `KeepRegions` not checked, the datum itself refused in the UI, the Restore's check, and the tooltip of a base candidate without an action. Tests of 2026-11-05: engine tests 168 and 169 in `fix_actions.rs` (25 tests) and test 170 in `src/fixes/mod.rs` (3 unit tests); U-251 in `src/app/actions/datum-base.test.ts` (18 tests, 21 after the review), the cases of `datum_base_moved` and `datum_base_self` in `messages.test.ts`, E-166 in `repairs.spec.ts`, and E-108's row. Against the code of 0ae7337, the parts that hold today pass (the documents build as the tests state, 169's revert before `f-ext1` and `Keep this match` without it, U-251 (h), and E-108's row in Chromium). The new behavior fails as expected. Built on 2026-11-05. The rule lives in the engine's `src/datum_base.rs`. `BaseRule` reads the motion of the old base once, when another feature uses a sketch of the datum plane. It refuses a base whose motion differs or is unknown. Both motions come from the datum plane as the registry builds it with that base alone, in the state just before it in its run, with no recompute (engine.md section 3.22 says so now, and so does ADR 0055's amendment). An open transaction keeps the recipe it began with (`OpenTransaction::began`). The old base is read from that recipe. `attach_plane` answers a datum plane with the rule. `offers` leaves out a `UseReferences` or `KeepMatch` at `/base/face` whose edits store a refused base. A cause leaves out a revert to one. The protocol schema and `ui/src/generated/protocol.ts` changed in the three descriptions of ADR 0016's entry. In the UI, `src/app/actions/datum-base.ts` asks `attachPlane` before a pick of an edited datum plane's Base plane field lands, refuses the datum itself with `datum_base_self` without asking, and checks a Restore's saved base first. A Choose menu pick lands in input order. OK waits for it as for a click. A Restore sends a saved face with the Top plane's placement as its fallback, which the core does not read for a datum plane (ui.md section 3.7). E-166 expected no `inspector` after OK. The selection of 'Datum plane 2' keeps the inspector open in selection mode. E-166 now checks that no `inspector` has `data-mode="command"`, as other tests of an edit do. Checked here: the engine's tests (271 unit and 492 integration tests), workspace clippy, the schema check, the CLI's examples, the Python tests, `pnpm test` (238 files), lint, typecheck, and the whole e2e suite in Chromium (234 tests) on the rebuilt web core. Only Chromium ran here. Review fixes of 2026-11-05: the Restore of a datum plane's edit stays closed when the core stops while it asks for the saved base, as it does when `set_focus` finds no core (U-251 (n)). ui.md section 3.7 now says that the Restore's hint names a saved face "this face". No pick tells which feature made it (U-251 (m) has a face case). A candidate of the base's repair that the core leaves without an action shows `datum_base_moved` as its tooltip, with the candidate's description as the plane (ADR 0055's choice 8, U-251 (o)). protocol.md's `invalid_params` row names a datum plane whose own record does not validate. Two layout slips in the engine's comments are fixed. Checked after the fixes: rustfmt, clippy of the engine and the protocol, the engine's tests (271 unit and 492 integration tests), `pnpm test` (238 files, 5121 tests), `pnpm test:scripts`, lint, typecheck, and E-165, E-166 and E-108's row in Chromium. The Rust edits of the review are comments only. The web core built at 19:26 still matches the engine.
- link-writes: the CLI's `export --output` and `schema --write` fail with exit 3 onto a link whose target is missing and keep the link, Python refuses a read-only document or export file with `PermissionError`, and the CLI refuses every read-only file it would replace (document, export or schema file) by the owner's rule (cli.md and python-api.md open issues 12, PY-1). Spec of 2026-11-05: cli.md section 3.7 (one write for `fmt`, `export` and `schema --write`: a link that leads to no file and a read-only file are refused with plain words, the look through a link, and a temporary file's error names the path as given), section 4's three rows, tests 66 to 71, open issues 12 and 13 resolved and open issue 14 (the read-only refusal) closed; python-api.md 3.1, 3.7 and section 4, test 124, test 123 (d) changed to point at it, open issue 12 resolved; desktop.md 3.4 step 4.1 (`link_target` looks through the link first), a section 4 row, test 73, open issue 14 resolved; file-format.md 3.11 names Python's refusal; ADR 0037's amendment of 2026-11-05 with four choices of the owner's rule. Built and reviewed on 2026-11-05: PY-1's status lines of that day.
- messages: the wordings of features.md open issues 14 and 15, features/fillet.md open issue 5 and recompute.md open issue 9, with the message lint and the tests that pin them. Built on 2026-11-05: a fillet, chamfer or shell whose search found no useful limit reads `..., and no radius near it does.` (`distance`, `thickness`). `EndOutside` reads `which now ends outside the body`. `Through` keeps its text, which is true for the cuts that record it. A length within the tolerance of its bound gets the bound the check applies (`Distance must be at least 0.000001 mm, but it is 0.0000001 mm.`), with the digits that tell value and bound apart in every unit (features.md 3.4 and 3.12). A bound's written number never lies on the side its bound refuses, strict bounds included (`greater than 0.000000058 deg` for the revolve's 1e-9 rad), unless a strict bound equals its limit to 15 significant digits (the countersink's `16 mm`). Fillet test 21 pins its whole message, and extrude test 46 its inch text. [ADR 0056](../adr/0056-range-messages-state-the-bound-the-check-applies.md) records the decision and five choices of the owner's rule: which values get the new bound, how a bound is written, the revolve's angle, `BothSidesZero` and `ConeRadiiZero`. No example's expected results changed.

The engine's web rows follow (18 rows, test 155). The five open issues and recompute.md open issue 4 are resolved. Speed work is later work.

Legend:
- **Sizes:** S is one small PR. M is several PRs in one area. L spans hosts or crates and needs a spec round.
- **Spec first:** the spec text that must merge before any code.
- **Human gate:** a license, dependency, schema, public API or capability change that waits for review (AGENTS.md Process).

These claims were spot-checked against the repo while planning, and all held then; the item after each has since fixed it:
- The only branch is `claude/tender-sagan-4ek456` (B-1).
- There is no notices file at the root (B-2).
- `ui/src/files/recovery.ts` has a single `SLOT = 'current'` (B-3).
- `ui/src/core/api.ts:223` sends `assets: []` (B-5).
- `scheduleRecovery` in `ui/src/app/createApp.ts` never checks `transactionOpen` (B-4).
- `Viewport.tsx` shows "Restart core" only when the status is `failed` (B-7).
- `ui/index.html` has no color-scheme meta (UI-1).
- `ui/playwright.config.ts` defines only a chromium project (B-9).

## 0. Fold into the reference stability plan now

These two are gaps in `docs/design/reference-stability-and-errors.md` section 10 itself. Fix them in the plan text, not after the phases.

**R-1. Mirrored drawing API** (partsmith-sketch)
- **Spec:** sketch.md 1.1, 2.2, 3.17, 6.21 (tests 248 to 250).
- **Spec first:** no spec change, the spec is already written. Plan text only.
- **Work:**
  - Add a phase 2 step before 10.4 step 1: `SketchParams::mirrored`, `mirrored_point` and `mirrored_region`, tests in a new `tests/mirrored.rs`, and the interface in the crate's AGENTS.md.
  - Cite that step from 10.4 step 1 (`TurnDrawingBack`, features/sketch.md test 24) and step 3 (engine `apply_fix`, engine.md test 135).
  - Add sketch.md tests 252 and 253 to 10.3 step 2.
  - Give test 251 (NC08 `NotConverged`) to the phase 3 sketch messages step.
- **Impact:** low, but phase 2 cannot exit without it. **Size:** S for the plan, M for the code.
- **Depends on:** `mirrored_region` needs phase 1 step 2 (`RegionRef.outline`, `RegionLoop`).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done in reference stability phases 1 and 2; nothing is left. The plan holds the mirrored drawing in section 10.3 step 2, a phase 1 step, rather than in a phase 2 step that 10.4 cites: that step names `SketchParams::mirrored`, `mirrored_point` and `mirrored_region` with tests 248 to 250, 252 and 253 and names phase 2's `TurnDrawingBack` (features/sketch.md test 24, engine.md test 135) as what needs it, and test 251 goes to phase 3 step 3, as the work list asks. Code: `crates/partsmith-sketch/src/mirror.rs`, named in the crate's AGENTS.md and called by `TurnDrawingBack` (`crates/partsmith-engine/src/fixes/frames.rs`). Tests 248 to 250 are `tests/mirrored.rs` and test 251 is in `tests/diagnosis.rs`; tests 252 and 253 check region matching, not the mirror, and are in `tests/boundary_matching.rs` and `tests/benchmark_scenarios.rs`, although the plan's sentence reads as if they were in `tests/mirrored.rs` (the plan is finished, so its text stays). Run here: those tests (25), features/sketch.md test 24 (`feature_sketch::t024`, 3) and engine.md test 135 (`fix_actions::t135`, 12) pass.

**R-2. P-9 belongs to no phase** (ui)
- **Spec:** ui.md 2.18, 5, 6.7 P-9.
- **Spec first:** no.
- **Work:**
  - `ui/e2e/fixtures/part-300.ptsm` does not exist.
  - `ps:drop-check` is missing from `ui/src/performance/marks.ts` `TIMING_NAMES` and nothing emits it.
  - Add both, and test P-9, to the phase 4 ui step next to the drop warning.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** phase 4 E6 (`list_uses`) and B-11 (the shared perf spec file).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done in reference stability phase 4, where the plan's section 10.6 step 3 puts it; nothing is left. `ui/e2e/fixtures/part-300.ptsm` holds the 300 features of ui.md 6.2 (60 groups of a sketch, an extrude, a fillet, a face sketch and a datum plane), `TIMING_NAMES.dropCheck` in `ui/src/performance/marks.ts` is `ps:drop-check`, which `src/timeline/use-strip-drag.ts` measures on each pointer move of an item drag, and P-9 is in `ui/e2e/perf.spec.ts`, tagged `@perf`. Run here: the mark's Vitest tests (`src/timeline/drop-warning.test.tsx` and `src/performance/marks.test.ts`, 17 tests) pass, and P-9 passed in Chromium with `PARTSMITH_PERF=1`; only a run on the reference machine decides its 1 ms budget (ui.md 6.7).

## 1. Blockers for a usable M1 release

B-1, B-2 and B-3 can start alongside the phases, since they do not touch phase code. B-1 and B-2 also need a human now.

**B-1. CI has never run: no `main`, no deploy, no release dry run** (repo setup, `.github/workflows`)
- **Spec:** delivery.md 1, 3.1, 3.3, 3.6, 6 (tests 1 to 13), 8. Technical design 4 and 17.
- **Spec first:** small. Add "create `main` and make it the default branch" to delivery.md 8.
- **Work:**
  1. Human setup:
     - Create `main` from the current head and make it the default branch.
     - Protect it, requiring occt-native, occt-wasm, kernel-wasm, rust, deny, ui, schema, core-wasm, web, e2e, python and desktop.
     - Create the Pages project with production branch `main`.
     - Add the `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` secrets.
  2. Open a PR into `main`. That gives the first full CI run and a preview URL (tests 1 to 12).
  3. Merge it. That gives the production deploy and saves the Linux caches on `main`.
  4. Dispatch release.yml from `main`. That runs test 13 and saves the Windows and macOS caches.
  5. Record the outcome in delivery.md open issue 1, and fix whatever the first runs expose.
- **Impact:** blocker. **Size:** M. The setup is small, but the fixes the first runs need are unknown.
- **Depends on:** a human repository owner. Every item that adds a CI job depends on this one.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** the human setup of step 1 is done, apart from branch protection. `main` is the default branch, the Git-connected Pages project has its own builds paused and takes CI's Wrangler deploys, and both Cloudflare secrets are set. The owner made the repository public and accepted ADR 0034 option A, B-2's notices and `check:dist` rule are on `main`, and no guard holds deployments back (the preview access policy and the production hold-back that ADR 0034's recommendation named as interim guards for the private period are not set up; production first deployed before the source stamp of delivery.md 2.13 reached `main`). After four red runs on pushes to `main` (38431025954, 38446384392, 38492295214 and 38857833462; the yanked `zip` 9.0.1 that failed deny in the last one is now 9.0.3), CI passed on `main` in run 38939853875 (commit 55dde8a, the push that brought reference stability phase 1 to `main`), and its `deploy` job made the first production deployment. That covers step 3 without the pull request of step 2: no pull request has run CI yet, so no preview exists. `main` is not protected yet; now that it is green, the jobs can be required. Step 4, the release dry run, waits for the AppImage choice of ADR 0034 (delivery.md 3.6, checklist item 2: the run's public `bundle-Linux` artifact would hold the AppImage), then step 5. delivery.md 8 records this. Every job sets `timeout-minutes` (150 for the OCCT builds and the release `bundle`, 60 for the others, 20 for the nightly; delivery.md 2.4 and 2.5, test 49), so a hung job ends long before GitHub's default of 360 minutes; the values come from the measured runs (cold OCCT builds of 56 and 88 minutes, no other job above 16). On the public repository, standard runners bill nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** CI has passed on every push to `main` since 55dde8a (13 runs); the latest, 40589977321 (cae269a, wave B, finished at 22:02 UTC), passed all 13 jobs, `deploy` included, so production deployments now carry the source stamp `/SOURCE.txt` of delivery.md 2.13, which `deploy` requires. Still open for the owner: `main` is not protected (the branches API reports `protected: false`), no pull request has run CI (the repository has none), so no preview exists, and release.yml has never run, so steps 4 and 5 still wait for the AppImage choice of ADR 0034.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left before the owner's steps, which are still open: `main` is not protected (`protected: false`), the repository has no pull request, so no preview exists, and release.yml has never run and no release exists, so steps 4 and 5 wait for ADR 0034's AppImage choice. CI has passed on every push to `main` since, the latest in run 41103065188 (228ad1d). Step 5 then fixes what the dry run finds.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** the owner's decisions settle what steps 4 and 5 waited for. The AppImage leaves M1 (ADR 0034 choice 1). Wave F's appimage track builds that, with item 2 of the release checklist gone from `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` and delivery.md 3.6. The release policy of delivery.md 1.2 and 3.6 is accepted as written: a dry run before each tag, unsigned bundles, macOS arm64 only, and drafts that a human tries and publishes. What is left is the owner's: protecting `main` (the branches API still reports `protected: false`) with a tag ruleset for `v*`, and the release dry run of step 4 once the AppImage change and a passing platforms run are on `main`, then step 5. CI passed on `main` in run 41785469932 (0724374). The repository still has no pull request. No preview exists.
- **Status (2026-11-04, appimage):** the AppImage is out of M1 (ADR 0034 choice 1, recorded in its amendment of 2026-11-04). `bundle.targets` in `tauri.conf.json` and the Linux row of `release.yml`'s matrix build the `.deb` and the `.rpm` only. The release checklist's AppImage item, item 2, is gone from delivery.md 3.6 and from `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`, and the later items are renumbered (the owner's leave of 2026-11-04). delivery.md tests 81 and 82 (`ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`) pin the release matrix and check that the checklist and the unsigned-bundle paragraph read the same in delivery.md, `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` and README.md. What step 4 waits for is the owner's: this change on `main`, a platforms run that passed on that commit (checklist item 2), then the dry run itself (test 13). That run is the first to show that `bundle-Linux` holds no `.AppImage`.
- **Status (2026-11-04, appimage review):** review findings 1 to 3 fixed. Two references still named the platforms requirement as checklist item 3 after the renumbering: delivery.md 2.5 and section 8's entry on the platforms workflow. Both now name item 2. delivery.md test 83 (`ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`) lists every reference by number to the checklist in delivery.md and `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` with words of its item. It fails when a reference names another item. ADR 0034 and its log row name the removed item as the checklist's AppImage item, not by a number that now means another item. The list of items that wait for a human, under Suggested sequence, now says it is the list from before the owner's decisions of 2026-11-04 and points at what still waits.
- **Status (2026-11-05, decisions):** the owner accepted the build tools pinned here and the new actions as built: `tauri-cli` 2.12.2, Wrangler 4.149.0, maturin 1.15.0, pytest 9.1.1, and `upload-artifact`, `download-artifact`, `setup-python`, `wrangler-action` and `msvc-dev-cmd` (delivery.md 8). The Cloudflare Pages project stays on the owner's account. The owner gave wave G's workflows-docs track leave to apply delivery.md 8's wording to `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`: the dependency archives, the dry run's archives job, the Windows import check, the static runtime rule and `full-rebuild`, updated for wave G. Production serves 0ae7337. What is left is the owner's: protecting `main` with the CI jobs required; a tag ruleset for `v*`; the release dry run of step 4 with its install checks; then step 5.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache spec):** the apt cache of the Tauri Linux packages is specified, with no code yet (delivery.md 2.2, 2.4, 2.5, 2.14, 3.2, 3.6, 5, tests 130 and 131 changed, the new tests 132 to 134, and open issue 16; ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choice 8, and its log row). Checked first: `rust`, `desktop` and `release.yml`'s Linux `bundle` row install the Tauri packages with no cache and no step timeout, and the bundle's install also holds `ninja-build`. In the CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369) the Rust job's install took 26 min 26 s at 29.8 kB/s, the first record that open issue 16 waited for, while `desktop` fetched the same 61.2 MB in 7 s. By the owner's rule, wave H3 caches them now. `rust` and `desktop` share one entry. The release bundle restores it and saves none, and `ninja-build` gets a step of its own that runs only on an OCCT miss. Every apt cache's key now rolls: a run on `main` saves the installed versions under its run id when the install downloaded a package the entry lacked, and a restore takes the newest entry. The Tauri installs get no step timeout. In this container, apt 2.8.3 wrote an epoch's colon as `%3a`, as `dpkg-query` with `sed` names the files, and the copy out of 3.2 passed five scratch cases with the real `dpkg-query`.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache tests):** the review's fixes are in the spec. The copy out starts with `set -o pipefail`. GitHub runs a step with no `shell` as `bash -e {0}`, and without it a failing `dpkg-query` would pass with an empty list (choice 8 (f)). After a hit whose install apt or dpkg fails on a cached file, every entry of the set is deleted. After a hit that ends at a timeout, none is (3.2, open issue 16). The promotion rule's exception in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` names a Tauri install that runs into its job's 60 minutes, and section 5 leaves out a run whose Tauri install took more than 10 minutes (choice 8 (h)). Open issue 16 records CI run 30 (42222611157): all 20 jobs passed in 15 min 7 s, and each browser entry is within 1 percent of its install's `Need to get` size. Test 131 now expects the branch run of wave H3 to restore those three entries. The release's copy step keeps `runner.os == 'Linux'` in its `if`, as every step of `bundle` for one OS does. Tests 130 and 132 to 134 are in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, with no workflow change yet: 35 tests, 20 of which fail until `ci.yml` and `release.yml` change. Against a scratch copy of the workflows changed as 3.2 says, all 210 tests of the file pass and the four workflows parse as YAML.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache implementation):** built. In `ci.yml`, `rust` and `desktop` run the five steps of delivery.md 3.2 around `Install the Tauri Linux packages`: in `rust` right after `Free disk space`, in `desktop` right after the restore of `.occt/native`, and in both right before the toolchain step. The install keeps its name and its command and has no step timeout. In `release.yml`'s `bundle`, the three steps right after checkout restore the Tauri entry, copy it into apt's cache and install the four packages of `rust` and `desktop`, each on Linux only. `Install Ninja` comes right after the OCCT cache step and runs only on Linux after an OCCT miss. A comment above each Tauri install says that its time is apt's download when the cache misses. `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` changed in the two sentences that 3.2 gives and in no other. All 494 tests of `pnpm test:scripts` pass, and the four workflows parse as YAML. In this container, `rust`'s copy out ran with `bash -e` and the real `dpkg-query`, with a scratch folder in place of apt's cache. On a miss it kept the two `libxdo` files that apt downloaded there (with `%3a` for the epoch) and left out a `libssl-dev` file of a version nothing installed. After the copy into a second scratch cache, `apt-get install --reinstall --download-only` reported `Need to get 0 B/91.3 kB of archives.` and the copy out wrote no output. After apt downloaded `ninja-build` too, it wrote `downloaded=true` and kept three files. Only CI can run test 131: the misses on the branch, the first save on `main`, the second save of the shared key, and the first hit.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache review):** two review findings fixed in the spec, the workflows AGENTS.md and the tests, with no workflow change. The copy out keeps a restored file whose package the runner image already holds at that version: dpkg lists that version as installed. delivery.md 3.2 said an entry stays about the size of its install's download, and test 131 called an entry more than 10 percent over its install's total a defect. 3.2 now says an entry also keeps such files. Test 131 checks that a save after a hit is at least 90 percent of its install's total and leaves out the versions the update replaced. Under the owner's rule the files stay: apt skips them, and leaving them out needs dpkg's list from before the install in every run (ADR 0038's choice 8 (i), Accepted (owner's rule of 2026-11-05)). A new case of test 134 pins it. In this container the copy out of `rust`, run with the real `dpkg-query`, kept the file of `bash` from the entry and dropped an old `coreutils` version. The promotion rule's exception in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` gave the 12 hours of delivery.md 2.13 to the Tauri installs too, and 3.2's Timeouts did not. The 12 hours apply to jobs that upload OCCT's compiled code, and `rust` and `desktop` upload no artifact. The exception now keeps them in parentheses for `e2e` and `e2e-engines`, and 3.2 says they do not apply to `rust` and `desktop`. The new test 135 checks the two sentences that 3.2 gives and those parentheses, and fails against the earlier sentence. All 498 tests of `pnpm test:scripts` pass.
- **Status (2026-11-06, wave H3 records):** CI run 30 is the first CI run on `main` after the move of the platform jobs. Its wall time replaces delivery.md section 5's estimate (test 129). Its saves showed none of the defects of test 131. Its Tauri installs took 45 s in `rust` and 31 s in `desktop`. The CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369) took 32 min 10 s of wall time (12:31:21 to 13:03:31), 26 min 26 s of it in `rust`'s Tauri install, and does not count against section 5. Its browser installs missed the cache, and their copy and save steps were skipped. delivery.md section 5 and open issue 16 hold the record.

**B-2. Third-party notices and OCCT LGPL compliance** (repo root, desktop bundle, web build, release)
- **Spec:** technical design 3 and 17, ADR 0003, LGPL 2.1 section 6.
- **Spec first:** yes.
  - A new delivery.md notices section, with section 6 tests.
  - The desktop.md `bundle` object, plus the exact-object test in `desktop/src-tauri/tests/config.rs`.
  - An ADR for the generator tool, or a row in ADR 0020.
  - A new ADR that resolves a contradiction: AGENTS.md and technical design 3 say OCCT is "linked as a separate library", but ADR 0003 links it statically. Either the wording or the linking changes.
- **Human gate:** licenses and a new tool.
- **Work:**
  - Collect the notices:
    - OCCT's `LICENSE_LGPL_21.txt` and `OCCT_LGPL_EXCEPTION.txt` from `$OCCT_ROOT/share/doc/opencascade`.
    - A statement that OCCT is statically linked, with where its source is and how to relink it.
    - The licenses of the Cargo.lock crates, per platform.
    - The licenses of the npm runtime dependencies.
    - The two self-hosted fonts of ADR 0031, both SIL Open Font License 1.1 with no Reserved Font Name: Geist 1.800 ("Copyright (c) 2023 Vercel, in collaboration with basement.studio", `ui/public/fonts/Geist-OFL.txt`) and JetBrains Mono 2.304 ("Copyright 2020 The JetBrains Mono Project Authors", `ui/public/fonts/JetBrainsMono-OFL.txt`, shipped as a subset). Each license text already ships next to its font in `ui/dist/fonts` and so in the desktop bundle (ui.md 2.16.7); the notices list both.
  - Ship them:
    - In desktop `resources` and `licenseFile` (desktop.md package test 49 can check the .deb).
    - Next to the web core, checked by check-dist.
    - As a release draft asset next to SHA256SUMS.
- **Impact:** high. Distributing without these breaks the LGPL. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** the human license gate, and B-1 for the CI checks.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** on `main` since the push of 55dde8a, which brought reference stability phase 1 there too, as the landing order asked; ADR 0034 decided, ADR 0033's gate open. Spec: delivery.md 2.9, 2.6, 2.5, 2.13, 3.6, 4, 6 (tests 6, 8, 12, 13, 34 to 42 and 52 to 59), 7 and 8; desktop.md 2.8, tests 49 and 53; ADR 0033 Proposed, ADR 0034 Accepted. Code: `ui/scripts/notices.mjs` with `pnpm -C ui notices` and `pnpm -C ui check:notices` (tests 35 to 42); the committed texts under `ui/scripts/notices/` (Apache-2.0 and MPL-2.0 from SPDX License List 3.29.0, ten copies from crate repositories at the commits their packages name, OCCT 7.9.3's license, exception and four bundled notices, Rust 1.97.0's Unicode-3.0 text, Emscripten 4.0.15's six texts and the WebView2 SDK license); both notices files, the desktop one with 362 crates and the web one with 54, each with the 11 npm packages that ship, generated twice byte for byte the same; the notices rule of `check:dist` (test 34); the bundle resource in `tauri.conf.json` (tests 33 and 53 pass); `check:notices` in the `schema` job; the notices check in the `desktop` job's package check (test 12, run against built `.deb` files); and the notices as a `draft` release asset covered by `SHA256SUMS`. Found while implementing: the icon `dist/esm/icons/copyright.mjs` of `lucide-react` matched the nested-file rule, so input 3 now leaves out JavaScript, TypeScript and source map files; the objc2 repository's only license file names MIT without its text or a copyright line; `webview2-com-sys` 0.39.1 ships the loader of SDK 1.0.3800.47, not 1.0.3650.58 as ADR 0033 said (now corrected there, with how it was matched and why the package's `NOTICE.txt`, for its `wv2winrt` tool, is left out). Found in review: `rolldown`'s `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSE`, the MIT notices of rollup and of esbuild, whose runtime helpers start `assets/index-*.js`, matched no prefix of input 3, so input 3 now also takes names starting with `THIRD-PARTY`, `THIRD_PARTY` or `THIRDPARTY` (test 38), and both notices files gained that text on rolldown's entry and changed nowhere else; the schema job now installs the packages before the schema steps and runs `check:notices` only when the install passed (test 50); and a change to the toolkits or options of `scripts/build-occt.sh` or `partsmith-occt-sys/build.rs` now repeats the OCCT notice search (2.9, input 4 and "Kept current"), which no check enforces. `ui/AGENTS.md` lists `notices`, `check:notices` and `check:dist`. The owner made the repository public and chose ADR 0034 option A on 2026-10-16. Its follow-through is written: the source stamp `/SOURCE.txt` that the `web` job writes into every deployment and `deploy` requires, the OCCT source archive `occt-7.9.3-source.tar.gz` that `draft` attaches to every release, made by `scripts/occt-source-archive.sh` from the tag and the commit `scripts/occt-version.sh` pins, with the same commit check after `scripts/build-occt.sh` clones (delivery.md 2.13), the notices statements that name them (2.9; both files regenerated, each changing only that paragraph), README.md's OCCT sentences (2.12 part 7), and the web notices in the `core-wasm` artifact with 7-day `bundle-*` and `playwright-report` artifacts (2.4, 2.5). Tests 52 to 55 and 57 to 59 pass in `pnpm test:scripts` (124 tests), and a test failed for each line of the stamp step, its check, the archive script, the commit check of `scripts/build-occt.sh` and the artifact settings that was broken on purpose, apart from `gzip -n`, which GNU gzip 1.12 does not need for piped input. Run here on 2026-10-16: the archive script on the local clone of `V7_9_3` and on a fresh clone of the upstream tag wrote the 17,457,358 bytes and SHA-256 of 2.13, also under `core.autocrlf=true`, `tar.umask=0077` and a global `*.cmake export-ignore`; the stamp step on a shallow fetch of merge commit 5a6a193 named its two parents and its tree; `pnpm build`, the stamp as CI writes it, `pnpm check:dist` and the deploy job's check passed. Test 56 passed: OCCT built from the extracted archive alone, without a clone, for native and for WebAssembly (Emscripten 4.0.15), and `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys` passed against each install (472 tests native; 467 on `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, 2 ignored). The pin changes the OCCT cache keys, so the first CI run with it builds OCCT cold once. - **Status (2026-11-04, strict-source spec):** later on 2026-11-04 the owner chose to ask no lawyer and to take the strictest reading of LGPL 2.1 sections 4 and 6 for each of ADR 0034's four questions. The lawyer's questions 2 to 4 leave B-2's human gate. The strict path is specified, with no code yet. Checked first: questions 1 and 4 are met already. Every deployment holds `occt-7.9.3-source.tar.gz` (the web-source track) and every release carries it (`draft`, delivery.md 2.13). Neither the notices nor any other text relies on a written offer. Question 2 (delivery.md 2.13, The dependency source archives): `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` makes, from `Cargo.lock` and `ui/pnpm-lock.yaml`, an archive of the Rust crates (`cargo vendor --locked --versioned-dirs`, with the crates outside the artifact left as stubs that Cargo needs to resolve the lock file) and one of the npm packages (the registry's tarballs, checked against the lock file's integrity), in parts of 24 MiB. `web` writes the web scope's archives into `/dependencies/` of every deployment with a `SHA256SUMS`. `deploy` refuses a build without them. `draft` attaches the desktop scope's archives to every release. Measured here: the whole lock files are 80,576,888 bytes of crates and 423,154,971 bytes of npm tarballs. The web scope is 5,104,811 and 83,374,080 bytes (54 crates, 147 packages). The desktop scope is 49,493,719 and 133,816,320 bytes (362 crates, 160 packages). Two packs from separate `cargo vendor` runs wrote the same SHA-256s. With no route to the internet, the web core, `pnpm install` and `pnpm build` ran from the extracted web archives alone. `cargo build -p partsmith-desktop` ran from the desktop crates. Question 3 (delivery.md 2.9 input 6): the tauri-bundler 2.10.2 that `tauri-cli` 2.12.2 pins downloads NSIS 3.11 (byte for byte the upstream release) and `nsis_tauri_utils` 0.5.3. Its installer script calls the plugins System, nsDialogs, StartMenu, NSISdl and `nsis_tauri_utils`. The desktop notices gain three Windows entries with their texts (tauri-bundler's are copies of the crate's own two license files). Nothing else the bundle formats add is missing from them. Tests 8, 13 and 40 change. Tests 89 to 98 are new. The test author's critic round tightened the spec: the archives cover the crates and packages that the app's executables and the web build are compiled from; the script exports a defined library (2.13) and the crate set comes from `scopeCrates` of the notices generator; for `web` it fails when a crate built for the Linux build machine is outside the web crate set; `deploy` also requires every part to be listed and the joined parts to make whole archives; the `web` step exports `LC_ALL=C` on its own line; `lint` reaches the script from the repository root (oxlint and oxfmt refuse `..`); the statements name the platforms and say to set `CARGO_HOME` to a full path; and test 97 builds the Linux bundles with `cargo tauri build`. New and proposed, needs human review (delivery.md section 8): the source of NSIS's LZMA module, which CPL-1.0 asks a distributor to say how to get (open issue 10); the tools that build the artifacts; what the archives give out under licenses no check has seen; the static Microsoft runtime in the Windows executable; and Partsmith's own source in each deployment. The strict reading also points at option (b) for CI's artifacts. That stays the owner's choice (open issue 15). No schema, protocol, capability or dependency change.

- **Status (2026-11-05, decisions):** the owner decided every part of the gate left open after wave F. Every CI, platforms and release run that uploads an artifact with OCCT's compiled code also uploads, in the same run and with the same retention, OCCT's source archive, the crate and npm source archives of its scope and Partsmith's own source archive (delivery.md open issue 15, option (b) widened). **B-3. Recovery keeps one slot, so unsaved work is lost** (`ui/src/files/recovery.ts`, `ui/src/app/actions/document.ts`, `ui/src/app/createApp.ts`)
- **Spec:** ui.md 2.15 (line 1852), 3.19 (lines 3032 and 3043-3044); desktop.md line 590 and open issue 7.
- **Spec first:** yes.
- **Work:**
  - Move to IndexedDB version 2, with entries keyed per document session and a migration of `current`.
  - A `replaced` stash survives later autosaves and saves of other documents.
  - A save clears only its own entry.
  - Startup offers every entry.
  - Tabs and desktop instances never overwrite or "Discard" each other's live copies.
  - Call `navigator.storage.persist()`.
  - Correct desktop.md's "at most 2 s lost" claim.
  - New U-tests: replaced, then autosave, then save; and two sessions at once.
- **Impact:** high (data loss). **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** nothing. Do it together with B-4. B-5 later adds asset bytes to these entries.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** done in the UI wave of 2026-10-13. IndexedDB version 2 keys recovery entries by id and moves the `current` record over (`ui/src/files/database.ts`, U-155 and U-170). Web Locks keep pages from offering, overwriting or discarding each other's live copies, and the first write asks once for `navigator.storage.persist()` (`recovery.ts`, U-156). The `replaced` stash survives later autosaves, a save removes only its own entry (U-157), two pages run at once (U-158), and startup offers every entry (U-159). desktop.md no longer claims "at most 2 s lost"; its open issue 7 states what two instances share. The tests pass (Vitest, `src/files` and `src/app`, 113 tests).

**B-4. The recovery copy stores uncommitted preview edits** (`ui/src/app/createApp.ts`)
- **Spec:** ui.md 3.19 against 4.4 (line 3253).
- **Spec first:** yes. One sentence: the recovery copy is the settled recipe.
- **Work:** have `scheduleRecovery` reuse `followSettledRecipe` so it never writes `transactionOpen` edits. Add a U-test next to U-77.
- **Impact:** medium. Today, crash recovery can reopen a preview as committed features, including a preview that caused the crash. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** do it with B-3.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done in the UI gap wave of 2026-10-13. ui.md 1.3 defines the settled recipe and 3.19 makes it the recovery copy: a `document_changed` with `transactionOpen` true neither writes nor delays the entry. `followSettledRecipe` in `ui/src/app/createApp.ts` follows that recipe, and `ui/src/app/recovery-copy.ts`, which took over from `scheduleRecovery`, writes it through `settledRecipeOf`. U-153, next to U-77 in `createApp.test.ts`, passes: a preview neither writes nor delays the copy, and a canceled command never stores its preview.

**B-5. Web and desktop hosts ignore document assets** (`ui/src/core/api.ts`, `ui/src/files`, the open, save and restart actions, `ui/src/test/fakes/core.ts`, `desktop/src-tauri/src/files.rs` and `commands.rs`)
- **Spec:** engine.md 3.14, protocol.md commands 6 and 7, file-format.md 3.11, technical design 6.

  - ui.md: `CoreApi` (lines 374-375), 3.19 (Open reads the sibling `assets/`; Save and Save as write the assets as file-format.md 3.11 says (assets first, a wrong entry replaced, then the document, then `markSaved`); on the web without a parent folder, either a directory pick or a notification naming the asset), 4.4 (restart keeps the assets), and new U- and E-tests.
  - desktop.md 1.1, 2.6, 3.2 to 3.4 and 3.7: a command and grant rule for the granted document's sibling `assets/` folder, with the matching `COMMAND_NAMES`, permissions and capability.
- **Human gate:** the capability review, including the two desktop options of ADR 0036.
- **Impact:** medium, but it breaks an M1 feature. Every document with an `import` feature, including those Python writes, opens with `asset_missing`, and Save as silently drops the assets. **Size:** L.

  - B-3 (the recovery entry format).
  - PY-1 (spec the asset write order once, for every host).
  - EX-1 (a real STEP asset to use as an E-test fixture).
  - Batch the desktop command review with B-6.

**B-6. Save overwrites a file that changed on disk** (`ui/src/files/file-service.ts`, `desktop-file-service.ts`, `document.ts`, desktop `files.rs` and `commands.rs`)
- **Spec:** ui.md 3.19; desktop.md 2.x (`read_file` returns the modification time).
- **Spec first:** yes.
- **Work:**
  - Remember `lastModified` (or mtime or a content hash) at open and at save.
  - On a mismatch, warn with Overwrite, Save as, or Open the newer file.
  - New U- and E-tests.
- **Impact:** medium. A git pull, a Python `Document.save` or another tab is silently discarded today. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** share the desktop command change and its review with B-5.
- **Status (2026-10-23):** code done (`src/files/digest.ts`, the file reference's `digest`, and Save's change check with `save_conflict` and its three actions in `src/app/actions/document.ts`); U-216, U-223 to U-225, U-86 and E-152 pass; review finding 2 fixed: writes run one at a time (`writeInTurn` over `createOneAtATime` in `src/core/queue.ts`), each built when its turn comes, and the change check reads the digest from the file reference as it runs, so a second Save during a save shows no false `save_conflict` (ui.md 3.19, U-223 (f)). Spec and tests were written before the code: ADR 0037 decision 1 compares a SHA-256 of the bytes Partsmith last read or wrote, taken with Web Crypto, with the file read again right before Save writes anything. Modification time and a host-side compare and write are listed as options. No desktop command changes: the check reads through `read_file`, so B-6 waits for no human review (ADR 0037 decisions 1, 7 and 8 need none). ui.md 2.8 `FileReference.digest`, 2.15 `fileDigest` and `readHandle`'s mode, the recent and recovery entries' `folder`, the recovery entries' `digest`, 3.19 (the change check, `save_conflict` with "Overwrite {file}", "Save as" and "Open the newer file"), tests U-216, U-223 to U-225 and E-152, U-86 amended (the check's read comes between `saveDocument` and `write`), and open issue 38.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** on `main` since wave A (2a202ac, 2026-10-24).

**B-7. No way out of a core stuck in a long call** (`ui/src/app/createApp.ts`, `ui/src/viewport/Viewport.tsx`, `ui/src/core/transport.ts`)
- **Spec:** technical design 14 ("never freezes"), ui.md 2.5, 3.21, 4.4.
- **Spec first:** yes.
  - ui.md 3.21 and 4.4: a "still working" state after N seconds, with "Stop and restart core". It terminates the worker, drops the queued mutations with a notice, and reopens the settled recipe without the open command.
  - desktop.md: an open issue, until request cancellation (later in protocol.md) lands.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** B-4 (settled recipe) and B-3 (the recovery entry survives the restart).
- **Status (2026-10-16):** done. After 10 s without an answer, the status bar and the viewport say the core is still working (U-161, U-171, U-172). "Stop and restart core" terminates the worker, drops the waiting changes with a notice and reopens the settled recipe without the open command (U-162, U-165, U-166, U-169). The desktop shows the panel without the button, and desktop.md open issue 9 explains why until request cancellation lands.

On Windows, `scripts/build-occt.sh` builds OCCT with `/MT`, `.cargo/config.toml` builds every crate with `crt-static`, and the shim follows the Rust target. The step `Check the imports of the app` runs `dumpbin -dependents` on `partsmith-desktop.exe` in `rust-platforms` and in the Windows `bundle` job, and fails on a DLL of the Visual C++ Redistributable (delivery.md tests 99 to 103). Tests 99 to 101 run on Linux. Test 102 is CI's alone: the first platforms run after the merge builds the Windows OCCT cold (about 48 minutes) and checks the debug app. The next release dry run checks the release app. Test 103 is a check by hand here: the Linux kernel tests on the OCCT install as it is, and a web core with the same SHA-256. Every OCCT key hashes the script. The first CI run after the merge also builds the Linux and WebAssembly OCCT cold (56 and 88 minutes on 2026-10-13). `main` builds them cold once more when the change merges through a pull request. A pull request's caches stay with it. Every Rust cache key changes once too (`Swatinem/rust-cache` hashes `.cargo/config.toml`). Only Windows compiles every crate again. The test author's critic round tightened the spec: the check fails on every DLL of the Visual C++ Redistributable (`^(msvcp|msvcr|vcruntime|concrt|vcomp|vccorlib|vcamp|mfc|mfcm)[0-9][^ ]*\.dll$`, ignoring case), not on `msvcp_win.dll` or `msvcrt.dll` of Windows; dumpbin's exit status is not used, and a missing `dumpbin` fails with `Imports not checked`; the texts say the Visual C++ runtime and the C++ standard library are static and the app takes the Universal C Runtime from Windows, while the CLI and the other test executables link it statically (delivery.md 3.6 item 4, kernel.md open issue 14); a local Windows OCCT install is rebuilt from an empty build folder; no crate that links OCCT has a doc test (delivery.md 3.6); the Windows Sandbox check first looks for the redistributable's DLLs and installs from the dry run's `bundle-Windows`; test 99 compares the Windows call with the Linux run's call; and test 61 reads its disk step as the last step.

Read here: OCCT 7.9.3's CMake files set no runtime and ask for CMake 3.10. The `WebView2LoaderStatic.lib` of `webview2-com-sys` 0.39.1 is compiled with `-D_MT` and carries no `RuntimeLibrary` check. The next release dry run from `main` checks the release app. The first CI run after the merge builds the Linux and WebAssembly OCCT cold as well (56 and 88 minutes on 2026-10-13). With Cargo 1.97.0 a flag in a target's `rustflags` does not reach a doc test. One in its `rustdocflags` does (checked here with a scratch crate). Doc tests now link the static runtime like every other executable. The rule on code blocks is gone from `partsmith-occt-sys`'s `AGENTS.md`, kernel.md 3.12 item 5 and delivery.md 3.6. Test 99 checks both entries. The Windows Sandbox check of desktop.md 6 names `msvcp140.dll`, `vcruntime140.dll` and `vcruntime140_1.dll` exactly: .NET Framework 4.8 puts `msvcp140_clr0400.dll` and `vcruntime140_clr0400.dll` in System32. The old pattern would have found them. The comment in `build.rs` is one line above `build.compile`. desktop.md 8 and delivery.md open issue 1 (e) tell the old runtime in the past tense. Whether the app and the executables the platforms run tests take one Universal C Runtime is proposed, needs human review (delivery.md 8, options (a) to (c)): the CLI and the kernel and engine tests link the SDK's UCRT statically, while the app calls the user's `ucrtbase.dll`. Checked here: `pnpm test:scripts` (299 tests), test 99 failing against the configuration without `rustdocflags`, `pnpm lint`, the four workflow files parse as YAML, `cargo fmt`, workspace clippy, `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys` (138 unit and 464 integration tests, no doc test) and test 103 for the web core: `scripts/build-wasm.sh` gives the same bytes from this tree, from this tree with each of `.cargo/config.toml` and `build.rs` back at HEAD, and from both back at 0724374 (`partsmith_core.wasm` `d310c45f...`, `partsmith_core.mjs` `796e4a56...`). The change leaves the web core as it was. The `9fe68bf4...` that the track recorded for the `.wasm` (the file on disk at the start of the review, built at 15:13) does not come back from any of these trees. Its cause is not known. The `.mjs` matches.

**B-9. The UI is tested only in Chromium** (`ui/playwright.config.ts`, `ui/e2e`, delivery)
- **Spec:** ui.md 1.1 and 4.3 (lines 3154-3155); desktop.md line 9.
- **Spec first:** yes.
  - ui.md 1.1 and 6: the supported browsers and webviews, with minimum versions, and which E-tests run on each engine.
  - delivery.md 2.4 and 6: install and run firefox and webkit, at least a smoke subset with `fileSystemAccess` false.
- **Impact:** medium. Two of the three desktop webviews are WebKit. **Size:** M, or L if WebKit needs fixes (Popover API, `@starting-style`, wasm exceptions).
- **Depends on:** B-1.
- **Status (2026-10-24):** spec and code written, the first run next, with B-8's (ADR 0038, proposed; the two browser builds and the minimum versions need human review). ui.md 1.1 lists the supported browsers and webviews with minimums from the features the UI needs (Chrome and Edge 114, Firefox 128, Safari 17.0; WebView2 114, the WebKit of Safari 17.0 on macOS 13 or later, WebKitGTK 2.44), and 4.3 keeps its texts, which already name those browsers (open issue 40: no startup check for the minimums). ui.md 6.8: `ui/playwright.config.ts` always defines the `firefox` and `webkit` projects with Playwright's `default: false`, so `pnpm e2e` still runs Chromium alone, each running the 24 tests tagged `@engines`, which run as written in every engine, with `fileSystemAccess` false or no file picker; E-156 (the projects) and E-157 (the subset each project lists, which launches no browser) are new. The `e2e-engines` job of the platforms workflow (delivery.md 2.14) runs them weekly in Playwright's Firefox 157.0 and WebKit 27.2; WebKit on macOS is ui.md open issue 41. Code: the three projects of ui.md 6.8 in `ui/playwright.config.ts`, the Chromium executable path moved into the `chromium` project, and `e2e-engines` in `platforms.yml`. Verified here, where only Chromium is installed: E-156 and E-157 pass, the 24 tests of the subset pass in Chromium (`pnpm e2e --project chromium --grep @engines`), the `firefox` and `webkit` listings each hold those 24, and `pnpm e2e` still runs Chromium alone with every test. README.md now names the supported browsers and their minimums (delivery.md 2.12), the only place a user learns them until ui.md open issue 40's startup check exists. The runs in Firefox and WebKit are the first platforms run's.

WebKit passed them in the third run (40509561577) and failed one in the fourth (40589987147, cae269a), with no change to `ui/src` or the test between the two: E-13 (`sketch.spec.ts`, Extrude from a sketch) found no `body-size` within 5 s after clicking Body 1, and the other 23 passed. Its log, read through the GitHub connector, shows only that wait (`element(s) not found`, `sketch.spec.ts:153`); the run's `playwright-report-webkit` artifact holds the trace, so whether wave B or a flaky wait caused it is open, and the WebKit row is eligible only after two passing runs in a row again.
- **Status (2026-10-28, E-13 cause and fix):** a product bug, not a flaky wait. An Enter (or an OK click) that arrived after the render showing an expression field's result and before React ran that render's passive effects found the field still pending, so `confirmCommand` returned without committing and the Extrude command stayed open over the preview's Body 1. Delaying `MessagePort` delivery by 60 ms in Chromium made E-13 fail every time with the same error. `ExpressionInput` now reports each state in a layout effect, so the owner hears it in the commit that shows it, and a value typed on the viewport marks its field pending until it is evaluated (ui.md 2.13 and 3.7, with timing tests in `ExpressionInput.test.tsx`, `Inspector.test.tsx` and `command-preview.test.ts`). The next platforms run on `main` checks WebKit.
- **Status (2026-10-30, input timing, spec and tests):** three timing leftovers of the confirm race fixes (a63f1e7, 722e2a1), specified in ui.md 3.5, 3.6, 3.7, 3.16.1 and 3.20 and decided in ADR 0050 (accepted, no human gate), no product code yet. E-89 failed under a page script that delivers every core worker message 60 ms late (kept under `target/tmp`, never committed) because a click right after Escape was picked against the model before the cancel's answer brought the cube back, and because "Select Cube" landed before the core answered the canvas click; clicks and selection changes now land in the order they were made, what reads the selection waits for them, and a click made while a command's end is on its way is picked once that end has settled, with the camera kept at the click. The other ways into a sketch, the click and double-click on the model included, start one at a time with feature commands from the input that asks for them, and Esc, the contexts and Finish sketch wait for a start in progress; Enter and `Mod+Z` wait for a click on its way. The Vitest regression tests are U-241 to U-245 (ui.md 6.4), each holding a core answer with a deferred promise; those marked guard pass on the current code, the others fail on it. E-89 stays as it is and is to pass under the harness once the fix is in.
- **Status (2026-10-30, input timing, implementation):** done. `src/app/actions/selection.ts` lands clicks on the model and selection changes in the order they were made (`landInTurn`, `inputLanded`), the registry commands that read the selection set `readsSelection`, `src/app/actions/starts.ts` runs feature starts, edits and every way into sketch mode one at a time (`startInTurn`) and Esc, Enter, the Model and Print contexts and Finish sketch after a start in progress (`afterStarts`), `commandEnding` in `command.ts` makes the viewport pick a click made during OK or a cancel once it has settled, with the camera kept at the click, and Enter and `Mod+Z` wait for a click on its way. An Enter pressed while a start is in progress counts as OK running from its turn after the start rather than from the key press, since the start would wait for that OK (ui.md 3.5, ADR 0050). U-241 to U-245 pass, as do the full Vitest suite, lint, typecheck and the Chromium e2e suite. Under a scratch late-message harness (every core worker message delivered to the page 60 ms late, strictly in order, four browsers at once; never committed), E-89 passed 12 of 12 runs on this tree and failed 11 of 12 on the build of ef7aef1, before the fix, with the two failures ADR 0050 names ("Made by Extrude 1" missing after the click right after Escape, and "1 face" in place of "Cube"). Not run here: Firefox and WebKit (the platforms workflow).
- **Status (2026-10-30, input timing, review fixes):** a review found that what ends a feature command still raced the clicks made into it, and that a right click during an end was decided too early; each is fixed and held by Vitest tests that answer the pick after the next action (U-246 in `src/app/actions/command-end.test.ts`, each case also run with the pick answered first as its guard, and U-242 (j) and (k)), all of which fail on the code before this pass. A start (a feature key, Sketch, re-attaching) ended an open command before the click into it had landed, so Shell, a click on a face and Sketch added a sketch on the face, and Chamfer, a click on an edge and F opened the Fillet with the edge, where a fast core cancels with Restore: `startInTurn` now runs every start once the input made before it has landed (the per-start waits it replaces are gone). Esc, Cancel and the close button ended the command at once and the click then landed in the app selection: `pickAt` now lands nothing once its command has ended (`commandLasts`). The Model and Print contexts and a `show` of another feature canceled without the click in the command, so offered no Restore: they now wait for the input made before them. A right click right after Esc or OK found nothing on the model drawn before the end's answer: it is now decided once the end has settled, with the camera kept at the click (`afterCommandEnd` in `controller.ts`, which also replaced `clickAt`'s `inTurn` flag with the `ClickLanding` it passes). ui.md 3.5, 3.6, 3.7 (step 1, Confirming, Canceling, Implicit cancel), 3.13, 3.16.1 and 6.4 and ADR 0050 (decision 5, still accepted, no human gate) say so; 3.7's Confirming now says an Enter that waits while a start is in progress counts as OK from its turn after that start, as 3.5 does. Lint, typecheck, the full Vitest suite (234 files, 5031 tests), the scripts and workflow tests and the Chromium e2e suite (229) pass. Under a new scratch harness of the same kind (every core worker message delivered 60 ms late in order, four browsers at once, under `target/tmp` and deleted), E-89 passed 12 of 12 runs on this tree and failed 10 of 12 on the build of ef7aef1 ("Made by Extrude 1" not found). Not run here: Firefox and WebKit (the platforms workflow).

- **Status (2026-11-06, firefox spec):** the Firefox failure of platforms run 13 is specified, with no code yet (ui.md 6.3, 6.8, E-156, the new E-167 and open issue 41; ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choices 5 and 6, and its log row). Checked first: `playwright.config.ts` sets no Firefox preference, and `expectReady` fails with the unsupported page's text alone. The Firefox experiment (run 42188760747) found a cause that fits it. Firefox waits at most 4 s for its startup graphics probe. On a fresh runner the probe can take longer to load Mesa from a cold disk (2 of 88 first launches). That browser then refuses WebGL2 for its whole life. The `firefox` project sets `webgl.force-enabled` and stays headed under `xvfb-run` with `retries: 0`. At the unsupported page, `expectReady` asks a new canvas for WebGL2 and adds the `statusMessage` of its `webglcontextcreationerror` event to its failure. A CI run on the branch and the first runs on `main` check the fix.
- **Status (2026-11-06, firefox tests):** the review's fixes are in the spec (ui.md 6.3, 6.5 E-2 and E-167, 6.8 and open issue 41; delivery.md 2.14; ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-06, choices 5 and 6). By the owner's rule, `expectReady` asks a new canvas for WebGL2 at the unsupported page, as the experiment's page did, in place of an init script in `openApp` on every page (choice 6). An empty `statusMessage` reads `no reason given`. A canvas that gets a context ends the message with `A new canvas got a WebGL2 context.` The startup check of `smoke.spec.ts` now waits with `expectReady`. Every subset test that starts the app then names the reason. 6.8 no longer says the preference changes nothing with a probe in time: the rows `force-2` and `force-8` that measure it have no result yet. It also says the preference turns off Firefox's other refusals of WebGL (`dom/canvas/WebGLContext.cpp` of Firefox 157). The new helper `refuseWebgl2` holds E-2's stub. Tests: E-156 and E-167, two checks of `expectReady`'s other endings, the startup check and E-2, in `ui/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` and `ui/e2e/documents.spec.ts`. In Chromium, E-156, E-167 and the two checks fail as expected until the change lands, and E-2, E-157 and the startup check pass. All of them passed against a scratch build of the change, since removed.

At the unsupported page, `startupState` in `ui/e2e/helpers.ts` asks a new canvas for WebGL2, and `expectReady` adds the browser's reason to its failure (choice 6). - **Status (2026-11-06, apt-cache review):** two review findings fixed in delivery.md, with no workflow change. Test 131 looked for a `Cache Size:` line in the save's log. In the source of `actions/cache` v6.1.0 (commit 55cc834), the save on github.com prints `Sent <sent> of <size>` lines before `Cache saved with key:`. Only the restore prints `Cache Size:`. The earlier status line's check against v6.1.0 covered 3.2's log lines and missed this one. Test 131 now compares the save's `<size>` with the install's `Need to get` line, and the first hit's `Cache Size:` line with the save's `<size>`. Open issue 16 records the entry's size from the same line. The 10 percent bound holds here: 52 `.deb` files that apt downloaded into a scratch cache (`Need to get 19.7 MB`) made a tar of 19,763,200 bytes, and gzip made it 19,657,076 (this container has no zstd). Open issue 16 now also names the runner image's apt failover (actions/runner-images at be74a77, merged in September 2026): one retry with 15 s timeouts, then the next of three mirrors. It helps only when a mirror sends nothing for 15 s. A mirror that keeps sending a few kB/s never trips it. In `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs`, the two tests 130 that compared each job's six steps are now one test per job of `E2E_JOBS`. `pnpm test:scripts` passes 472 tests. The four workflows parse as YAML.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache spec):** the browser caches of `e2e` and `e2e-engines` take wave H3's key rule (delivery.md 3.2, tests 130 and 131, B-1). A restore takes the newest entry of the browser, wave H2's `playwright-1.64.0` entries included. A run on `main` saves a new one only when the install downloaded a package the entry lacked. The step `Read the Playwright version` goes, and the install steps keep their names and 10 minutes.
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache implementation):** built. `e2e` and both rows of `e2e-engines` restore the newest entry of their browser by the prefix `apt-archives-ubuntu-24.04-<browser>-`, copy it into apt's cache when the restore found one, and on `main` save a new entry under the run's id when the install downloaded a package the entry lacked (delivery.md 3.2, test 130). The step `Read the Playwright version` is gone. The install steps keep their names and their 10 minutes (test 79).
- **Status (2026-11-06, tauri-cache review):** a browser entry keeps the restored files of packages that the runner image already holds at those versions, as the Tauri entry does (delivery.md 3.2, test 134, choice 8 (i)). Test 131 no longer counts a save after a hit as a defect when such files make it larger than its install's total.
- **Status (2026-11-06, wave H3 records):** the CI run on wave H2's branch (42218307369) and CI run 30 (42222611157, on `main`) ran the `firefox` row with `webgl.force-enabled`. Its 24 tests passed in each, in 59.8 s and 1.2 min. The `webkit` row's 24 passed in 1.2 min and 55.2 s. These runs are the first CI runs' check of ADR 0038's choice 5. The case the preference exists for, a probe that timed out, still has only the 30 contexts of the Firefox experiment's `slow-probe-force` row (ui.md open issue 41). Each row's install missed the apt cache in both runs, and run 30 saved the first entries (delivery.md open issue 16).

- **Work:** implement it with `node:zlib`. No new dependency is needed.

- **Depends on:** nothing. The CI web job already runs `check:dist`.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** done. delivery.md 2.6 defines the limit (250,000 bytes, 1 KB being 1,000 bytes), what counts as initial (the scripts and `modulepreload` links of `index.html`, plus the core worker script), and gzip level 9; section 4 has its messages and tests 26 to 33 its cases; ui.md 6.7 points at it. `ui/scripts/check-dist.mjs` implements it with `node:zlib`, and `pnpm test:scripts` passes (37 tests). The headroom is delivery.md open issue 8: 94.1 percent of the limit on 2026-10-13, and 96.8 percent on 2026-10-16 (242,039 bytes gzipped, 7,961 under the limit, `check:dist` on a build of the tree then), against the 92 percent of the index row, which is the gap analysis's figure.
