## 2. After the blockers, by owner

Add examples after phases 1 to 3, so their expected results are blessed once, against the final message texts.

### Print (ui/src/print, partsmith-print, python reports)

**PRINT-1. Show split-plane and drain-hole suggestions**
- **Spec:** technical design 11; print.md 2.6, 3.9.2, 3.9.8.
- **Spec first:** yes. ui.md 3.17, python-api.md 2.9, and optionally cli.md 3.3.
- **Work:**
  - Add `suggestion` to `HealthRow` (health.ts lines 11-17), with row text such as "Split at x = 150 mm" or "Drain hole near (5, 5, 5)".
  - In `focusedFindingPoint`, mark `drain_hole` at `suggestion.at`.
  - Draw split planes in the viewport (`scene.ts`, `overlay.ts`), clipped to the body's box and framed when the row is focused.
  - Add `Finding.suggestion` in `python/partsmith/reports.py`.
  - Optional: a CLI text line (rebuild.rs lines 337-344), and a one-click route to `split_body`.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M, or L with the one-click split.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** implemented: `Suggestion::text` in `partsmith-print` and the CLI's `suggested:` line, `Finding.suggestion` in `partsmith.reports`, and in the UI `suggestion.ts`, the row's `health-suggestion` line, the drain hole marker, box framing and the split planes in the overlay pass. Checked against the repository first: `partsmith-print` already computes both suggestions and the protocol and `ui/src/generated` already carry them, so no schema changes; no host showed them (`HealthRow` has no suggestion, Python's `Finding` drops it, `rebuild --checks` prints only the message). ADR 0040 decides that `partsmith-print` words a suggestion in millimeters (`Suggestion::text`, the words of the new print.md 3.12), which the CLI prints, that Python and the UI word it themselves in the same words, kept in step by shared test cases (no protocol label, so no schema review and no expected result changes), and leaves the one-click route to `split_body` for an engine fix after M1 (ui.md 1.2). The track's paths therefore include `crates/partsmith-print` (`Suggestion::text` and its tests). Spec: print.md 2.6, 3.12, open issue 7 and tests 51, 52, 55, 56, 94, 113 and 114; cli.md 3.3 and test 63; python-api.md 2.1, 2.9, 3.7 and tests 104 to 106; ui.md 2.1, 2.16.4, 3.12 pass 7, 3.17 (rows, focus, split planes, the marker's lifetime), 1.2, 6.1, 6.2 (`hollow-resin.ptsm`), E-108, U-233 to U-237, E-158 and E-159.
- **Status (2026-10-27, review):** implemented and checked, waiting for human review of its Python API only. Review findings 1 to 4 fixed: the focused finding's marker and split planes also go when a new report replaces the one the finding was taken from, since a stale report's rows stay listed and the same key can name another finding in the next report (`focusOutdated`, ui.md 3.17, U-236); `findingAt` is imported from `src/print/checks.ts` only; the Python tests share `StubPart` and `only_finding` through `support.py`. ADR 0040 is accepted except its Python API (`Finding.suggestion`, `Suggestion` and `SplitPlane` in `partsmith.reports`), which is a public API change and so proposed, needs human review, with options (a) to (d) and (a), as implemented, recommended.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0040's Python API (`Finding.suggestion`, `Suggestion` and `SplitPlane`, options (a) to (d)) is open.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0040's Python API with option (a), as built. The one-click route waits for an engine fix after M1 (ui.md 1.2).

**PRINT-2. Custom printer profile route, or mark it Later**
- **Spec:** print.md 1.1, 2.2, 4; protocol.md command 4.
- **Spec first:** yes, as a decision. Either:
  - a CLI `--printer-file` on `rebuild` and `export` (cli.md 2, 3.3, 4, 6), plus a Python profile argument or `Document.add_printer` mapping `invalid_profile` and `profile_id_reserved` (python-api.md 1 to 3); or
  - an explicit "Later" in cli.md 1 and python-api.md 1.
- **Human gate:** public API, with an ADR entry.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S to mark Later, M to implement.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** decided "Later". Checked against the repository first: a document stores only its printer's id (`printer`, file-format.md section 3.2), so the format carries no custom profile and a route would have to bring the profile along on every run; no host sends `put_printer_profile` (ui.md 1.2 already lists custom profiles as Later). cli.md section 1 (Later) and its `--printer` row, python-api.md section 1 (Later), print.md section 1.2 (Later) and protocol.md section 2.5 command 4 say so, without naming a route. ADR 0047 decision 2 records it, proposed, needs human review, with options (b) a CLI `--printer-file` and a Python profile argument or `Document.add_printer`, (c) profiles stored in the document and (d) profiles persisted by the hosts. No code or test changes.
- **Status (2026-10-28, implemented):** nothing to build, since the decision adds no code. Checked against the code: no host sends `put_printer_profile` (`python/`, `crates/partsmith-py`, `crates/partsmith-cli` and `ui/src` name it only in the generated types), and `check(printer=...)` passes the id as `analyze_print`'s `profile`, which fails with `unknown_printer_profile` for an id the session does not have (engine test 80), so cli.md 1, python-api.md 1, print.md 1.2 and protocol.md 2.5 command 4 describe the hosts as they are. Human gate open (ADR 0047 decision 2).
- **Status (2026-10-28, review):** done, waiting for human review. The review found no defect in this item: "Later" stands in cli.md 1 and its `--printer` row, python-api.md 1, print.md 1.2 and protocol.md 2.5 command 4, and matches the code. Human gate open (ADR 0047 decision 2, options (b) to (d)).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0047 decision 2 (options (b) to (d)) is open.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0047 decision 2 with option (a), custom printer profiles Later in the CLI and Python, as built.

**PRINT-3. Protocol schema describes listed printer profiles in their input form**
- **Spec:** protocol.md command 3 and 2.9; print.md 2.2, 3.1.
- **Spec first:** yes. Say which form each direction uses, and log it in ADR 0016.
- **Human gate:** protocol schema.
- **Work:**
  - Give results the written form (required fields, `FitClasses`). Requests keep `ProfileInput`.
  - Update `profile_schema_reads_the_input_form` and add a protocol schema test.
  - Regenerate `protocol.schema.json` and `ui/src/generated`.
  - Remove the workarounds in `printer.ts:106-114` and `bed.ts:90-92`.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** the phase 1 schema regeneration (PR1 to PR5), so both reviews do not touch the schema at once.
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** checked against the repository first: `PrinterProfile` takes its schema from `ProfileInput` through `#[serde(try_from)]`, so the one protocol schema (deserialize contract) describes listed profiles in the input form; requests already carry `ProfileInput`; the engine lists `PrinterProfile`s, which serde writes in full, so it needs no change; the workarounds are `printerDetail` in `printer.ts` (bed and nozzle) and `bedOutline` in `bed.ts`. Spec: protocol.md section 2.5 commands 3 and 4, section 2.9 and test 86; print.md sections 2.2 and 3.1 and test 124, which replaces the unit tests `profile_schema_reads_the_input_form` and `profile_schema_writes_every_field_but_the_optional_nozzle`; ui.md section 3.17 (`printerDetail`); ADR 0047 decision 1 with options (b) to (e) and a planned entry in ADR 0016, proposed, needs human review. The written form stays closed to unknown fields, as `$defs/PrinterProfile` was and as serde reads a profile. Work: `#[schemars(!try_from, deny_unknown_fields)]` on `PrinterProfile` and `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `FitClasses` (both checked with the locked schemars 1.2.2), `PrinterProfile` named beside `FixRef` in `crates/partsmith-protocol/AGENTS.md` among the result types that reject unknown fields, `partsmith schema --write`, `pnpm -C ui generate:protocol`, and the UI fallbacks for the always-present fields removed (`printerDetail` keeps leaving out an absent nozzle, the one optional field).
- **Status (2026-10-28, implemented):** `#[schemars(!try_from, deny_unknown_fields)]` on `PrinterProfile`, whose doc comment names the form its schema describes as print.md 2.2 does, and `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on `FitClasses` (`crates/partsmith-print/src/profile.rs`); the engine is unchanged. `partsmith schema --write` regenerated `docs/format/protocol.schema.json` (`$defs/PrinterProfile` in the written form with `id` the `Id` definition, and the new `$defs/FitClasses`; `ptsm.schema.json` unchanged) and `pnpm -C ui generate:protocol` `ui/src/generated/protocol.ts`; ADR 0016's planned row is now its 2026-10-28 entry. The UI's fallbacks went: `printerDetail` reads the bed directly and leaves out only an absent nozzle, and `bedOutline` is null only for a profile that is not installed (ui.md 3.17). `crates/partsmith-protocol/AGENTS.md` names `PrinterProfile` beside `FixRef`, and `crates/partsmith-print/AGENTS.md` says its schema is the written form. Protocol test 86, print test 124 and the protocol schema test 45 pass. Human gate open (ADR 0047 decision 1).
- **Status (2026-10-28, review):** done, waiting for human review. Review findings 1 and 2 fixed. 1: protocol.md sections 2.3 and 3.6 said clients ignore every unknown field in results and events, and the versioning rule of 2.9 called every added result field compatible, although results already carried closed types; they now say clients ignore unknown fields of the protocol's own result and event types, 3.6 names the closed types that results and events carry (`FixRef`, `PrinterProfile` with `FitClasses` and `Bed`, and the file-format types `Document`, `Parameter`, `Body`, `Reference`, `RegionRef` and `Placement`), and 2.9 says a field added to one of them comes with a new format version (file-format types) or is a breaking change (the others). The new protocol test 87 pins that list against the schema and checks that `decode_response_frame` ignores an unknown member of the `list_printer_profiles` result but rejects one in a listed profile; ADR 0047's Consequences note that this holds with option (e) too, since (e) would open the schema only, not the decoders. 2: the serde rejection of an unknown `FitClasses` field is now part of print.md test 124, and its test is `t124_fit_classes_reject_an_unknown_field`. Human gate open (ADR 0047 decision 1, options (b) to (e)).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0047 decision 1 (the protocol schema change logged in ADR 0016, options (b) to (e)) is open.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0047 decision 1 with option (a) and ADR 0016's 2026-10-28 entry, as built.

### Examples corpus (examples/, the per-feature tests in partsmith-recompute, the runner in partsmith-cli)

  - a kernel test in kernel.md 6 and `occt-sys/tests/exchange.rs`.
  - The examples themselves need no spec change.
- **Work:**
  - Add `examples/import/assets/<blake3>.step` (the 10x20x30 "Block") and new stems: step-block, join-, cut- and intersect-step-block. They have no Python twins.
  - Bless their expected results (volumes from import.md tests 1, 2 and 7).
  - Close delivery.md open issue 5, and fix the stale `asset_missing` text in import.md test 3 and section 4.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** nothing. It also supplies the fixture for B-5.
- **Status (2026-10-23):** done. No production code changed: the kernel and the feature already meet the new tests, and the four examples are blessed with the volumes of their tests. features/import.md tests 9 (intersect, `import/intersect-step-block`), 10 (`import_warning`, its text also run through the message lint) and 11 (tests 1, 2 and 7 on `import/step-block`, `import/join-step-block` and `import/cut-step-block`, and the asset's name checked against its BLAKE3 hash), kernel.md test 135, and delivery.md open issue 5 resolved. Tests 10 and 135 skip two free faces, not one: the kernel's one-shape text `Skipped 1 shapes that are not closed solids.` is not grammatical, so no test pins it; its fix is features/import.md open issue 3, now item IMP-1 (a human gate for its recommended option). The `asset_missing` text of import.md test 3 and section 4 was already current (commit 3f1bc05). Review fixes: the face checks of tests 1, 7 and 11 also check each face's normal (`assert_face_in_plane`, as test 9 does), `box_step_asset` moved to the import fixtures it builds on, and a committed asset that no example imports, which no runner test hashes, is left to EX-9.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** on `main` since wave A (2a202ac, 2026-10-24).

**EX-3. Sketch constraint and tool examples, plus an arc-bounded profile**
- **Spec:** technical design 16; sketch.md 1.1; ui.md 3.16.4 and 3.16.5.
- **Spec first:** only the arc-bounded profile, as a new extrude.md 6 test that `feature_extrude.rs` loads. It pins `f-ext1/lateral/<arc id>`.
- **Work:**
  - One small example per constraint missing from `examples/sketch`. Each extrudes the sketch, so the volume and bounding box pin the solved position.
    - No example at all: point_on_object, parallel, perpendicular, tangent, collinear, concentric, midpoint, diameter, angle.
    - Only in the benchmark: coincident, vertical, equal, symmetric, radius, horizontal_distance, vertical_distance.
  - The point-point and point-line distance forms.
  - Constraints that use `origin`, `x_axis` and `y_axis`.
  - The output of each M1 tool: constrained 2-point rectangle, center rectangle, circle with a point snap, 3-point arc, tangent inference, angle to X_AXIS, diameter.
  - Optional outcome examples: `sketch_conflict`, `sketch_invalid`, `sketch_not_solved`, and an open outline that the extrude reports as `profile_not_found`.
  - Slot and tangent-arc tools stay out, since they come after M1.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** L (about 20 examples).
- **Depends on:** phases 1 and 3 (region outline, sketch messages).
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec written and reviewed. As for EX-5, every example but three outcome examples is a section 6 test loaded through `Run::example`, so the solved positions are checked by the tests and not only by blessed files: features/sketch.md tests 34 to 50 and the three untested outcome examples (20 examples in `examples/sketch/`), and features/extrude.md tests 62 (`extrude/arc-bounded`, pinning `f-ext1/lateral/e-2`) and 63 (`extrude/open-outline` and `extrude/open-outline-connected`: NC01's sketch under an extrude picked before the gap, `profile_not_found`, then the region after Connect the ends). Tests 34 to 43 draw the geometry off parallel, perpendicular, collinear with the x axis, concentric on the origin, equal, symmetric about the y axis, the point-point and point-line distances, horizontal and vertical distance from the origin, and the radius of an arc, so the solve moves it. Tests 44 to 50 are the outputs of the M1 tools and also the examples of midpoint, point on object, tangent, angle and diameter, which no other example shows. The outcome examples `sketch/conflict` (NC07), `sketch/not-solved` (NC08) and `sketch/invalid-value` (a diameter parameter of 0 mm) have no tests of their own: the newcomer error corpus and test 11 test what they show, and their expected results pin the messages. 19 of the 23 examples have Python twins. Review fixes: "as stored" (bitwise) only where sketch.md 3.7.2 or 3.7.3 keeps an entity, "as drawn" (within 1e-9 mm) where the solve still adjusts it; the clicks and cursor sides that decide each tool's output, and test 49's 30 where the tool computes 29.999999999999996; test 63's connected document as its own example, and `P-RECT-V2` named; tests 51 to 53 dropped as repeats of NC07, NC08 and test 11. No production code, schema or dependency change is expected: the documents, rebuilt from scratch copies with the current CLI and Python bindings, give the specified results and twins. The implementer runs the libm perturbation check over the 23 examples before blessing and records the result in kernel.md open issue 14, as EX-2, EX-4 and EX-5 did; the Windows and macOS rebuilds of the new examples are checked only by the next `platforms.yml` run (delivery.md 2.14), not in this container.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done, on `main` since wave B (cae269a). No production code, schema or dependency changed: the solver, the profiles and the extrude already meet the new tests. The 23 examples have blessed expected results, and the 19 that qualify have their Python twins. `feature_sketch.rs` loads tests 34 to 50 through `Run::example`, and also checks that each of their examples stores its region as a pick of the solved sketch. `feature_extrude.rs` loads tests 62 and 63. The outcome examples' expected results pin the messages of NC07, NC08 and test 11, and `extrude/open-outline`'s `profile_not_found`. `feature_support` holds `solved_point` and `stored_region_match`, which both files use, and `stored_region`, which `stored_region_match` and `feature_sketch.rs` read. The libm perturbation check moved none of their results in its ten modes, nor in two more that move every `atan2` result, and the sketch and extrude tests passed in all twelve, so no example was reshaped (kernel.md open issue 14). None of the 23 has a print finding. The Windows and macOS comparison of the 23 expected files runs only in CI, in the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).
- **Status (2026-10-27, review):** no correctness, determinism or spec coverage defects found; the examples, expected results and twins are unchanged. Review fixes: ui.md 3.16.4 now says that the examples of tests 44 to 50 are kept in step with the tools by hand, since no UI test reads them, and `ui/AGENTS.md` says so beside `recipes.ts` and `drawing.ts`, while a Vitest that replays the tools against them is a new EX-9 bullet; kernel.md open issue 14 no longer says the wrapper cannot move `atan2` (its two `atan2` modes moved all 36 of `shell/concave-edge-join`'s and left its overhang as it is, so only OCCT's own arithmetic stays out of reach); the three tests of the "as stored" and "as drawn" helpers left `feature_sketch.rs`, as the `#[should_panic]` helper test of EX-5 did, since they came from no spec test; and `assert_volume_faces_and_bounds` in `feature_support` now checks a body's volume, face count and bounding box for tests 34 to 50, 62 and 63. The track's scratch files under `target/tmp` are deleted. The Windows and macOS comparison still waits for the next platforms run.

**EX-5. Feature option examples**
- **Spec:** technical design 16; the In M1 lists of the per-feature specs.

  - hole: arc start, end and center points; successful blind counterbore and countersink.
  - sketch: a sketch on YZ.

Reshape an example whose `/checks/` move in any mode; do not bless it. Record the outcome in the EX-5 status and in one sentence in kernel.md open issue 14.

- **Depends on:** phase 2 (axis `direction`, face placement) and phase 3 (hole points).
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done, on `main` since wave B (cae269a). The cone's join is its test 16 (`cone/on-face`), so there is no `cone/join`. circular_pattern test 18 builds `circular_pattern/six-around` again with a canceled token through `Run::assert_build_canceled`, which linear_pattern test 13 now shares. The libm perturbation check moved none of their results in its ten modes, nor in twelve more seeds of its sampled modes, so no example was reshaped (kernel.md open issue 14). **EX-6. Export coverage in the corpus**
- **Spec:** technical design 16 ("each import and export path"; the STEP round trip within 1e-6).
- **Spec first:** yes. delivery.md 2.8 and 6 must first say what an export example is. Either:
  - a runner test that runs `partsmith export --json` for stl, 3mf, obj and step and compares the report; or
  - a STEP export, import and volume check for every example that builds.
- **Work:** then implement it in `examples.rs`.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** EX-1 and B-1.
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** spec text written, no code yet; EX-1 and B-1 are done. The first form, which alone covers the four formats, is runner test 5 of delivery.md 2.8 ("Export examples", [ADR 0046](../adr/0046-examples-exported-to-every-format.md)): for every example that builds (its expected results have no feature with status `error` and a body without `"empty": true` that its `.ptsm` leaves visible and a part; 232 of 256), `partsmith export --json <example> -o <output>` to each of `stl`, `3mf`, `obj` and `step`, in a temporary directory, must exit with 0, and its report without `warnings` must match `{"files": [...]}` with the one file asked for, with `model/<format>` and the size of the file written, by the comparison rules of 2.8. Warnings are not compared (they come from the `standard` mesh whose problems the expected `mesh_validity` findings hold; `extrude/union-touching-itself` warns `non_manifold_edges` on purpose), nothing is stored or blessed, and `py` stays with the twins. delivery.md section 4 has three new runner rows, section 6 tests 68 to 70 check the parts, open issue 4 has the time and open issue 18 the STEP round trip not taken (option (c) of the ADR); cli.md 3.4 item 8 says what each `files` entry holds. Measured before any code with the current CLI: the 232 examples export to every format with exit 0 and a report that matches; the other 24 exit with 1 (`features_failed` for 7, `nothing_to_export` for 17); exporting the corpus took 41 s on 4 cores, against 24 s for the whole `examples::` module; and, by a temporary test, the 232 STEP files imported back gave volumes within 9.0e-13 relative of their expected bodies' sum and unchanged face counts. So no export defect shows and no production code changes. The work is `examples.rs` alone (runner test 5 and tests 68 to 70, with `run_each` generalized to argument lists and the rebuild's failure handling shared, so a rebuild's messages stay as they are), and the CLI's AGENTS.md names the export check. Windows and macOS run it only in CI, in the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).
- **Status (2026-10-28):** done here; Windows and macOS run it in the next platforms run (delivery.md test 64). `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/examples.rs` has runner test 5 (`examples_export_to_every_format`) and its helpers: `Example::builds` and `builds` (the rule of 2.8, from the expected results and the `.ptsm`), `EXPORT_FORMATS`, `export_output` (the example's path with `.` for `/`, so each example and format has its own file in the test's temporary directory), `export_arguments` and `check_export`. `run_each` takes argument lists, and the rebuild and the export share `printed_json` (the run starts, exits with an accepted status and prints JSON) and `reported_error`, so a rebuild's messages are unchanged but in one case that no run of the CLI is known to reach: a rebuild that exits with a status other than 0 or 1, prints nothing on standard error and prints JSON with neither `error` nor `features` once read `exited with N: the report has no features array` and now reads `exited with N: ` with nothing after it, since `exit_reason` reads only the JSON's `error` member, as section 4's rows say for both. All 73 tests the `examples::` filter selects pass in 55 s on 4 cores (24 s before), and the whole CLI crate's 163 integration tests in 64 s. A temporary one-byte change to the expected size gave exactly 928 problems, 232 per format in the order `stl`, `3mf`, `obj`, `step`, so every example that builds is exported and compared in each format. No export defect showed, so no production code changed, and no expected result changed. The CLI's AGENTS.md names the export check and the second run after a bless.
- **Status (2026-10-28, review):** two low findings fixed, no production code, schema, dependency, workflow or expected result changed. `Example::builds` read the `.ptsm` through `Example::document`, which panicked on a file that cannot be read, so runner test 5 stopped at it and lost every other problem; `read_json` now reads both of an example's files and gives `None` for a file that cannot be read or is not JSON, so such an example is not exported (runner tests 2 and 4 report it, delivery.md 2.8 "Export examples" now says so for the `.ptsm` too), and runner tests 1 and 3, which read the `.ptsm` the same way, no longer stop at it either. Regression tests: `an_example_whose_files_cannot_be_read_does_not_build` (it panicked before the fix), `a_file_that_cannot_be_read_has_no_json` (a directory, which no platform reads as a file) and `a_file_that_is_not_json_has_no_json`. delivery.md section 4's rebuild row now gives `{reason}` as the code has long written it (the standard error, else the JSON's `error`, else nothing) with the words of a run that cannot start or prints no JSON, and the export row refers to it; `exit_reason_of_a_rebuild_without_standard_error_or_an_error_member_is_empty` holds the case the earlier status line now names. Cleanup: `model_runs` builds the argument lists of runner tests 2 and 4 (`partsmith <command> <model>` for each example), which each test had spelled out since `run_each` took argument lists, with its own unit test; the arguments are unchanged. Windows and macOS still run runner test 5 only in the next platforms run (delivery.md test 64); a Windows export that fails there with exit 3 because a scanner holds the new temporary file would be fixed in the CLI's `files::write`, which unlike the desktop host (desktop.md 3.4 step 4.5) does not retry a rename.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the owner's question in delivery.md 8 ("Examples rules") is open: whether runner test 5 also imports each STEP file back and compares its volume (delivery.md open issue 18, ADR 0046 option (c)), about 20 lines in `examples.rs` if the owner wants it. ADR 0046 itself is accepted. Runner test 5 has passed on `windows-2025` and `macos-15` in the examples step of `rust-platforms` in the four platforms runs since it reached `main` (40935502508 on 722e2a1 to 41103071504 on 228ad1d), so the Windows rename failure the line above foresees has not shown.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** the owner chose ADR 0046 option (c). Runner test 5 also imports each STEP file it exports and checks its volume within technical design section 16's tolerance (delivery.md open issue 18). Wave F's step-roundtrip track specifies and builds it in `examples.rs`. The platforms run after that runs it on Windows and macOS.
- **Status (2026-11-04, spec):** spec text written, no code yet. delivery.md 2.8 has a new paragraph, "STEP round trip". Once an export to `step` passes the report check, runner test 5 imports the bytes it read with `import_step` into a new millimeter document of the headless session of the worker that ran the export, right after that export, and measures the body it makes. That volume must be within 2.8's tolerance for numbers that are not both integers of the sum of the expected volumes of the bodies the export takes (1e-6 relative for every volume of 1 mm³ or more). Section 4 has two new runner rows: `partsmith export to step wrote a file that does not import: {message}` and `partsmith export to step: the imported volume is {actual}, expected {expected}`. Test 107 checks the expected volume, the volume check, a file that does not import, the order of the checks, the file the binary writes and that file against another expected volume. Open issue 18 is resolved. **EX-7. Chamfer corner faces are never produced in any test**
- **Spec:** features/chamfer.md 1, 3.4; kernel.md 3.4.7.
- **Spec first:** yes.
  - A kernel test in kernel.md 6 and `blends.rs`: chamfer every BOX edge; each of the 8 vertices generates exactly one face.
  - A chamfer.md 6 test: CUBE20, all 12 edges, equal 2 mm. Expect status ok, 26 faces, 12 `f-cha1/chamfer/edge(...)` and 8 `f-cha1/corner/vertex(...)`, with an OCCT note like fillet.md open issue 2.
- **Work:**
  - `examples/chamfer/every-edge` with its twin, and a `feature_chamfer.rs` test.
  - Optionally, a `dressup.rs` unit test.
  - If OCCT does not report the corners as generated from vertices, fix `name_blend`.
- **Impact:** low. It may expose a silent naming defect. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** phase 2 (the chamfer side rule).
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** spec text written, no code yet. kernel.md test 142 (section 6, "Chamfer corners") chamfers every `BOX` edge at 1 mm and checks the history the naming reads: each edge generates its chamfer face (its centroid 1/2 into the box from the edge's midpoint along each of its faces' normals), each vertex its corner face (bounded by 3 edges and 3 vertices, its centroid 2/3 mm into the box), and no edge a corner face; kernel.md open issue 4 is now resolved: OCCT reports corners from their vertices for fillets and chamfers, so the shim adds nothing. features/chamfer.md test 19 is `CUBE20` with all 12 edges at 2 mm, as `examples/chamfer/every-edge`: ok, volume 7562.666667, 26 faces, the 12 chamfer and 8 corner names, each chamfer face's normal and centroid (all 12 have the same area, so only where a face lies tells a chamfer face named after another edge), and each corner face's centroid 4/3 mm from its own corner, so a vertex name given to another corner fails; chamfer.md open issue 3 is the OCCT note. Measured before any code with the current CLI and Python bindings on a scratch copy: names, areas and volumes are as specified and the print checks find nothing (the bottom chamfers face down at exactly 45 degrees, which print.md's `ANGLE_TOLERANCE` keeps within the limit), so `name_blend` needs no fix and no production code changes. The unit test `chamfer_corner_faces_come_from_the_vertices` of `src/blends.rs` checks part of test 142 on the same box, so it moves into `tests/blends.rs` as test 142 instead of staying beside it, and its helper `generated_faces`, which no other unit test uses, goes with it (left behind, it fails `clippy -D warnings` as dead code); the module doc comment of `tests/blends.rs` lists test 142. No `dressup.rs` unit test: test 19 checks every name and where its face lies. Test 19 fixes `chamfer/every-edge` as the EX-8 tests fix theirs, so EX-8's rule for the libm perturbation check applies to it: it is not reshaped. Before committing, rebuild the web core with `scripts/build-wasm.sh` (a module built from another tree fails engine.md test 154, which compares every example between the web core and native code), then run `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored` and `cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten` (test 142 also runs in CI's `kernel-wasm` job).
- **Status (2026-10-28):** done. No production code changed: OCCT 7.9.3 lists each corner face in its vertex's `generated` list, so `name_blend` needed no fix. kernel.md test 142 is the six `t142_*` tests of `tests/blends.rs`, which pass natively and under Node (`cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`); the unit test it replaces left `src/blends.rs` with its helper. features/chamfer.md test 19 is the three `t019_*` tests of `feature_chamfer.rs` on `examples/chamfer/every-edge`, which has its twin and blessed expected results with no print finding. The libm perturbation check moved none of its results (kernel.md open issue 14). The web core was rebuilt from this tree and engine.md test 154 (`web_parity::`) passes with every example, this one included. The Windows and macOS comparison of its expected file runs only in CI, in the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).

The scan needed the rule's exceptions written down, so features.md 6.1 now has a paragraph of its own, "Documents from examples", which decides them: a test that expects an error or a warning needs no example for the documents it also runs that build ok, the document after a fix (extrude.md tests 52 to 54 and 58, fillet.md tests 19, 20 and 22, chamfer.md tests 17 and 18, shell.md test 20, hole.md test 34, sketch.md tests 23 and 24) or a variant (sketch.md test 29 at 270 deg), and loads another test's example where that document is the other test's (boolean.md test 17 after Unsuppress, `boolean/join`), since such a document is what the fix or the variant checks; a document a test makes from its example in code (values, ids, members or records changed, added or left out, a member in place of another such as a translation made a rotation, a point moved, a list reordered, or a fix applied with the reference the run gives) needs none, and the spec says which document the example holds; nor do a test that builds nothing (sketch.md test 15), one that runs another committed document (shell.md test 23, the UI fixture `cube-features.ptsm`), features.md's own tests of sections 6.2 to 6.5 (`feature_messages.rs`), which check messages across feature types, or the nine tests of `feature_<type>.rs` that come from no spec test (the motion checks `a_beveled_face_moves_with_the_part`, `a_sketch_on_the_beveled_face_stays_on_it_after_the_move`, `a_rounded_face_moves_with_the_part`, `the_image_faces_move_by_the_reflection`, `the_rim_and_inside_faces_move_with_the_part`, `the_cut_faces_move_with_the_part`, `a_sketch_on_a_datum_takes_the_datums_motion` and `a_face_sketch_in_body_coordinates_takes_the_motion_of_its_face`, and `a_new_extrude_records_nothing_absent`), which a spec cannot name an example for, or the release-only budgets of `tests/budgets.rs` (linear_pattern.md test 14, hole.md test 33 and shell.md test 12 build from records), which time a document rather than check one that CI rebuilds (a fifth exemption of section 6.1, added by the review of this spec). datum_plane.md test 19 also makes from its example the document with the rotation and test 10's `f-dp2` added after `f-dp1`, which `t019_a_datum_on_a_datum_on_a_turned_face_passes_on_the_turn` runs today: the one check in which a datum passes on a motion other than the identity, so the spec now names it, and features.md open issue 22 with it. An example lives under `examples/<type>/` unless its document is one of `examples/references/` already or another test's. Each of the 39 tests now names its example. Ten documents are examples already: `boolean/join` (boolean.md 17), `datum_plane/chained` (the datum bases of datum_plane.md 19), `extrude/region-by-outer-boundary` (extrude.md 31), `hole/simple-through` and `hole/blind-flat` (hole.md 36), `linear_pattern/leaning-edge` (linear_pattern.md 17, whose example stores the region's `outline` and the axis's `direction`, as picks store them since format version 2), `references/moved-face-sketch` (sketch.md 20, move_body.md 10, hole.md 37), and `revolve/full-ring`, `revolve/full-angle` and `revolve/quarter` (revolve.md 27); no test of a spec loaded `region-by-outer-boundary`, `leaning-edge` or `moved-face-sketch` by name before (the examples runner and the twin checks run every example). The other 31 are new, each the document its test runs today, record for record, with its twin: `chamfer/join-renames-top` and `chamfer/join-behind` (15, 16); `circular_pattern/sketch-on-copy`, `circular_pattern/turned-edge-axis` and `circular_pattern/leaning-edge` (12 to 14); `datum_plane/on-split-face`, `datum_plane/on-shelled-top` and `datum_plane/on-moved-face` (17 to 19); `extrude/side-line-redrawn`, `extrude/back-line-redrawn-taller`, `extrude/side-line-split`, `extrude/cut-through-exactly`, `extrude/region-lines-survive` and `extrude/cut-ends-outside` (47 to 49, 51, 56, 57; test 57's reversed run loads test 51's example); `hole/from-inside-body` (36); `linear_pattern/turned-edge-direction` (18; test 16 loads `sketch/on-pattern-copy`); `mirror/sketch-on-image` (13); `move_body/leaning-edge` and `move_body/turned-edge-axis` (11, 12); `revolve/leaning-edge`, `revolve/turned-edge-axis`, `revolve/side-line-redrawn`, `revolve/side-line-redrawn-half` and `revolve/side-line-split` (28, 29, 31 to 33; test 32 loads test 31's example for its run after the first edit); `shell/walls-thicker-than-rounds`, `shell/rounded-cube`, `shell/arc-join-beside-round` and `shell/walls-just-under-rounds` (15, 19, 21, 22); `sketch/on-split-face` and `sketch/on-pattern-copy` (25, 27); `split_body/by-split-face` (12). Which document an example holds: for the four corpus R38 tests the leaning wall (`LEAN(-5)` in circular_pattern.md 14 and revolve.md 28, the point at (-1, 20) in linear_pattern.md 17 and move_body.md 11); for a test of an edit (extrude.md 47 to 49, revolve.md 31 to 33) the document after the edit with the references stored before it, as the `examples/references/` examples hold theirs (extrude.md 48 and revolve.md 32 after their second edit); for a test with and without a stored `direction` the document with it; otherwise the test's first document, the others made by the test from it. shell.md test 19 now lists the thicknesses its tests run, 1, 2 and 3, since the spec has to say which one the example holds (2, the wall of ui.md's fixture). No converted test picks the references of the document it loads, so no example stores picks and no pick test is kept apart: extrude.md test 47's KeepMatch document takes its edge from the run of the example, a second document. No production code changes, no new acceptance test and no ADR: the tests keep their assertions and the examples are the documents they check today, so nothing was measured for this spec. For the implementer, in this order: (1) write the 31 documents as the tests build them and switch each test to `Run::example` with every assertion kept, making its variants from the loaded example with `feature_record_mut` (in `dressup.rs`, as shell.md test 14's `without_the_move` does) and `features.retain`, and adding at most one helper, for the shared-point move of the four R38 tests (`e-3`/`e-4`, `e-12`/`e-13`); (2) run the libm perturbation check of kernel.md open issue 14 over the 31 (19 have curved faces whose mesh-dependent print findings may move: the cylinders and bores of the circular pattern, hole, mirror, sketch and extrude cut examples, the fillets of `extrude/side-line-redrawn` and `back-line-redrawn-taller`, the five revolves and the four shells) and record any result it moves in that open issue instead of reshaping the example, since the tests fix the documents; `shell/arc-join-beside-round` is the one new example whose risk only the platforms run shows, since its overhang under the step takes in the arc join's facets up to 45° and so depends on the 36.0 step count of `shell/concave-edge-join`, which no wrapper moves (shell.md tests 21 and 24); if the check moves no result, this item's status line records it (the modes, the seeds and that the 31 examples matched in every mode), since kernel.md open issue 14 is edited only for a result that moves; (3) run `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::` and check that the only new `.expected.json` files are the 31; (4) run `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1` pytest on `python/tests/test_examples.py` in the venv, which writes each of the 31 twins that is missing or fails check 1 or 4 (the Python bless decides from `<stem>.expected.json` which examples qualify, so it comes after the Rust bless; `partsmith export --format py` writes the same script); (5) run both runners again without `PARTSMITH_BLESS` (delivery.md 2.8); keep runner tests 6 to 9 and 74 to 78 passing; rebuild the web core with `scripts/build-wasm.sh` and run `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored` (engine.md test 154 compares every example); then mark features.md open issue 22 Resolved by EX-11, keeping its number, and drop the sentence `Open issue 22 lists the tests not yet converted.` from section 6.1. In the cleanup pass, delete the builders of `feature_support` whose only callers now load examples, `lean`, `lean_edge_axis`, `slotted_block` and `sketch_on_pattern_copy_document` of `frames.rs` among them (`#![allow(dead_code)]` keeps clippy from reporting them), and keep those that outside callers still use: `leaning_wall`, `leaning_wall_pattern`, `moved_box_document` and `box_top_sketch_document` (`format_version.rs`; `moved_box_document` also serves the warning tests) and `rounded_cube_document` (the shell budget of `budgets.rs`, at t = "2" and "12"). Docs that change with the code: `crates/partsmith-recompute/AGENTS.md` for the one new helper of `feature_support`, and the module doc comments of the `feature_<type>.rs` files that list the stems they load. With 31 examples, three times EX-8's, the item is nearer size L than M. The Windows and macOS rebuilds of the 31 expected files are checked only by the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).
- **Status (2026-10-30, implemented):** done. No production code changed. Each of the 39 tests of features.md open issue 22 loads its example (71 test functions, and sketch.md test 32 on test 25's example), the 31 new examples hold the documents the tests built, canonical and with their twins, and open issue 22 is resolved, its sentence gone from features.md 6.1. The libm perturbation check of kernel.md open issue 14 (a wrapper rebuilt for it, 24 modes: the Windows cosine; every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up, every `acos` up and every `pow` up; every `atan2` up and every one down; one `sin` or `cos` result in 4 with seeds 1 to 12 and one in 32 with seeds 1 to 6, chosen with their direction by its own hash) moved `fillet/every-edge` and `shell/moved-body` by their recorded lines and the results of one new example, `circular_pattern/leaning-edge` (features/circular_pattern.md test 14), in 15 of the 24 modes: the copy's bottom disc tilts and meets the bed beside one point of its rim, so its first-layer contact and the lower bound of its overhang are decided by the diagonals between the disc's cocircular rim nodes. The other 30 matched in every mode, `shell/arc-join-beside-round` among them, whose 36.0 step count no wrapper moves (shell.md tests 21 and 24), and the 513 tests of the 13 converted `feature_<type>.rs` files passed in every mode. The test fixes the document, so kernel.md open issue 14 records the result with its lines and the example is blessed as it builds on Linux; of the 31 it is the most likely to differ in the next platforms run, and a mismatch there goes to delivery.md open issue 1 (d), never to expected files for one platform. Only the 31 expected files were blessed (`PARTSMITH_BLESS=1` for the Rust runner, then for `test_examples.py`, which rewrote no twin), and both runners pass again without it, runner tests 6 to 9 and 74 to 78 among them. Cleanup: `lean`, `lean_edge_axis`, `slotted_block` and `sketch_on_pattern_copy_document` of `frames.rs` are gone, and `with_shared_point_moved`, the one new helper, is in `crates/partsmith-recompute/AGENTS.md`. The web core rebuilt from this tree is unchanged byte for byte, and engine.md test 154 passes with every example, the 31 included. The Windows and macOS rebuilds of the 31 expected files are checked only by the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).
- **Status (2026-10-30, review):** done; review findings 1 to 3 fixed, no correctness, determinism or weakened-test defect found. Finding 1: the sentence the implementation added to delivery.md 2.8 (a per-feature test that runs a corpus scenario keeps its document under its spec's type) left out the exception of features.md 6.1, by which sketch.md test 20, move_body.md test 10 and hole.md test 37 load `references/moved-face-sketch`, and delivery.md is not one of this item's paths, so the sentence is gone and delivery.md is as it was; features.md 6.1 alone holds the rule. Finding 2: the six tests that run their example without its axis's stored `direction` (circular_pattern.md test 13, linear_pattern.md tests 17 and 18, move_body.md test 12, revolve.md tests 28 and 29) each removed it with the same four lines, so `frames.rs` now has `remove_axis_direction(document, feature, path)` beside `with_shared_point_moved`, named in `crates/partsmith-recompute/AGENTS.md`; it removes the member with `shift_remove`, as the other test helpers do, which gives the same document here since `direction` is the axis's last member, and the six tests, whose results differ with and without the direction, are its regression tests. Finding 3: 12 of the 13 converted `feature_<type>.rs` files load the 31 new examples (`feature_boolean.rs` loads only `boolean/join`), so kernel.md open issue 14 and the line above now say the 513 tests of the 13 converted files. No example, expected file, twin, schema or production code changed, so nothing was blessed and the web core was not rebuilt; `cargo test -p partsmith-recompute` (lib 106, integration 1168), the examples runner (118, runner tests 6 to 9 and 74 to 78 among them), `test_examples.py` (1138 passed, 1 skipped) and engine.md test 154 pass. The Windows and macOS rebuilds of the 31 expected files are still checked only by the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14).

### Kernel exchange and the import feature (partsmith-occt-sys, partsmith-features)

**IMP-1. Import warnings show the kernel's sentences** (added on 2026-10-23 by the review of EX-1)
- **Spec:** features/import.md open issue 3; kernel.md 2.12 and 3.8.1 read item 3; features.md 4.2 (`ImportWarning`) and test 14; docs/guidelines/errors.md.
- **Problem:** `ImportWarning` shows each string of `StepImport::warnings` as it is. A STEP file with one free face beside a solid reads `Skipped 1 shapes that are not closed solids.`, and a solid that repair could not fix is named by its position in the file (`Solid 2 is not valid after repair; booleans with it may fail.`). Both are kernel text, which features.md test 14 keeps out of rendered messages, and the first is not grammatical. features/import.md test 10 and kernel.md test 135 skip two faces, so no test pins either text.
- **Spec first:** yes, one of these options:
  - (a) Reword only the kernel's two strings in kernel.md 3.8.1 read item 3, for example `Skipped 1 shape that is not a closed solid.` and a repair warning without the solid's number. No public API changes, but users still read kernel text, so features.md 4.2 still conflicts with test 14.
  - (b) `StepImport` returns counts (shapes skipped, solids still invalid after repair) and the feature words them, for example `This import left out 1 shape of the STEP file that is not a closed solid.`, with the kernel's text in the detail. It changes kernel.md 2.12 and 3.8.1 and features.md 4.2 together.
  - Either way, new tests for one skipped shape and for the repair warning in kernel.md 6 and features/import.md 6.
  - Recommended: (b), the only option that keeps kernel text out of messages.
- **Human gate:** public API, for (b) only (the fields of `StepImport` and `ImportWarning`), with an ADR entry.
- **Work:** `cpp/exchange.cpp` and `src/exchange.rs` in partsmith-occt-sys. For (b), also the `ImportWarning` rendering in partsmith-features and the texts pinned by features/import.md test 10 and kernel.md test 135.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S for (a), M for (b).
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** spec text written for option (b), no code yet (ADR 0045, proposed, needs human review, with the options still open). `StepImport` gains `skipped_shapes` and `invalid_solids` and keeps its sentences as technical text, the one-shape sentence now singular (kernel.md sections 2.12 and 3.8.1, tests 135 and 139 to 141, open issue 15); `ImportWarning { content: ImportContent, count, detail }` words the counts, at most one warning per kind, invalid solids first, with every kernel sentence in the detail (features.md sections 2.1, 2.8 and 4.2, test 18 and open issue 21; features/import.md sections 3.3 and 4, tests 10 and 12 to 14, open issue 3). `partsmith-io` passes `warnings` through as before (io.md section 3.8 now calls them technical text), so it, the engine, the protocol and the examples do not change. The repair fixture is `write_step`'s text of a box with a cavity whose void is listed twice (kernel.md test 140's `VOID_TWICE`, whose volume counts the cavity twice): of the defects tried, only that and a void that is its own outer shell stayed invalid after repair. The second has no volume, and a defect it showed in the engine, found while writing this spec, is now item ENG-7. kernel.md tests 139 to 141 are in `tests/exchange.rs`, so CI runs them natively (`rust`), under Node (`kernel-wasm`) and on Windows and macOS (`rust-platforms` of the platforms workflow); the spec's probes ran natively only, the implementer runs `source /opt/emsdk/emsdk_env.sh && cargo test -p partsmith-occt-sys --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten --test integration exchange::` here before the track is done, and Windows and macOS stay unverified until the platforms workflow runs.
- **Status (2026-10-28):** done here, waiting for the human gate of ADR 0045 (proposed, needs human review). `cpp/exchange.cpp` counts the shapes it leaves out and the solids still invalid after repair in `StepImportData` (two `usize` fields of the bridge, `src/ffi.rs`) and words one skipped shape in the singular; `StepImport` carries `skipped_shapes` and `invalid_solids`, with `warnings` documented as technical text. `partsmith-features` has `ImportContent` beside `IgnoredReason` (re-exported at the crate root), `ImportWarning { content, count, detail }` renders features.md section 4.2's four texts and returns its detail from `detail()`, and `builtin/import.rs` makes at most two warnings, invalid solids first, each with the detail `read_step: ` and every kernel sentence joined with spaces. The doc comment of `ImportedStep::warnings` (`crates/partsmith-io/src/step.rs`) and the AGENTS.md of `partsmith-occt-sys` and `partsmith-features` follow; no example's expected result, schema or protocol changed. Run here: kernel.md tests 135 and 139 to 141 natively and under Node (`exchange::` on `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, 42 tests, the native-only standard streams test left out), features.md tests 14 and 18, features/import.md tests 1 to 14 through recompute, the new unit tests of `src/exchange.rs` and `builtin/import.rs`, the examples, pytest and the Chromium e2e run on a rebuilt web core. Windows and macOS stay unverified until the platforms workflow runs. Review fixes: the invalid solids' message was true for op New only (with Join, Cut or Intersect the import's own boolean is the first to use the solid, and the join of `VOID_TWICE` onto test 7's plate builds with the cavity gone), so it now reads `..., so the shape this import gives may be wrong, and features that change it later may fail or give a wrong shape.` for every op, recorded under ADR 0045 option (b4) (features.md section 4.2 and test 18, features/import.md section 4 and tests 13 to 15); features/import.md test 15 pins it on that join and test 16 pins the import's warning before a body rule's (`separate_solid` of `ONE_FACE` joined apart from a box); an import whose own boolean fails still drops its warnings and names no invalid solid, now features/import.md open issue 4 (no input is known that makes it fail: `VOID_TWICE` joins, cuts and intersects the plate without an error), since naming it changes `FeatureError` or the kernel's hints; the recompute helpers that check each import message now fail when there is none, and recompute's copy of the `VOID_TWICE` edit checks, as the kernel's does, that there is one `BREP_WITH_VOIDS` and that its void is reversed.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0045 (the public API of `StepImport` and `ImportWarning`, and the options still open) is open. kernel.md tests 135 and 139 to 141 passed on `windows-2025` and `macos-15` in every platforms run since the import track reached `main` (40657874315, fba52b0, 2026-10-28), and features/import.md open issue 4 waits for an input that makes an import's own boolean fail.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0045 option (b) as built, with the public API of `StepImport` and `ImportWarning`. No human gate is left.

### Engine and WebAssembly (partsmith-engine, partsmith-wasm)

ENG-1, ENG-4 and SK-1 can share one wasm-target CI job.

**ENG-1. Native and WebAssembly results are never compared**
- **Spec:** engine.md 3.18 (line 514); recompute.md 3.9 (line 399).
- **Spec first:** yes. A new engine.md 6 test, a recompute.md test, and a delivery.md 2.4 row and section 6 test.
- **Work:**
  - Add a Node script in `crates/partsmith-wasm/tests/node` with a Rust driver like `web_module.rs`, gated by `PARTSMITH_REQUIRE_WEB_MODULE`.
  - Send open_document, recompute and measure through `ps_call` for the engine fixtures and `examples/**`, and compare with native `Session::call`: statuses, messages and ids exactly, floats within 1e-9 relative.
  - Let the test select `SequentialIds`, since the web session uses random ids (`lib.rs:21`).
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** B-1.
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0042, accepted). engine.md 3.18 now says what the builds share and what may differ (display buffers; recipes written from solved geometry agree in value, not text) and test 154 is the parity test: `crates/partsmith-wasm/tests/web_parity.rs` with `tests/node/parity.mjs`, ignored by default and run by `core-wasm` with `-- --ignored` after `scripts/build-wasm.sh` (delivery.md 2.4, tests 7 and 66). Its first draft sent `open_document`, `measure` of each body, `analyze_print` and `close_document` for the 8 engine fixtures and every example (a prototype run on 2026-10-27, 1,056 requests, matched every header except 7 findings' `triangles`; the meshes of 99 bodies differ, engine.md open issue 18); the spec as reviewed adds `list_elements` of each body (1,353 requests) and its own rule for descriptor geometry (see the review line below). Strings and integers exactly, other numbers within `max(1e-9 * |native|, 1e-9)`, arc angles modulo 2π, print reports within delivery.md 2.8's tolerance without `triangles`, buffers by count and element type. recompute.md 3.9 no longer promises equal keys and kernel results across platforms, and test 74 points at test 154. `SequentialIds` turned out not to be needed: none of these commands draws an id (only `import_step` and `apply_fix` do), so the native session takes the module's own options.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done, on `main` since wave B (cae269a). No production code changed and no test-only id mode added (ADR 0042 decision 4). The parity test, engine.md test 154 and recompute.md test 74, is `crates/partsmith-wasm/tests/web_parity.rs` with `web_parity/comparison.rs` and `tests/node/parity.mjs`, and `ci.yml`'s `core-wasm` runs `cargo test -p partsmith-wasm --test integration web_parity:: -- --ignored` after the `web_module::` round trips and before the upload (delivery.md 2.4, test 66). Run here against a web core built from this tree, it passes over the 8 fixtures and 245 examples in 56 s (debug build, Node 22); with a stand-in script that stops after three requests it fails with the specified `no response from the web core` line and keeps its two frame files. The AGENTS.md of `partsmith-wasm`, `partsmith-engine` and `partsmith-recompute` and `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` name the test and its command. Only CI shows the step on the runner and its time.
- **Status (2026-10-27, review):** done; review findings 1, 2, 4 and 5 fixed, finding 3 declined. The geometry of `list_elements` descriptors keeps 1e-9 relative with an absolute floor of 1e-8 instead of delivery.md 2.8's 1e-6 (engine.md 3.18, test 154 and open issue 18, ADR 0042 decision 1): with a floor of 1e-9 the test reports exactly the 4 numbers of open issue 18, at most 1.59e-9 apart, and with 1e-8 it passes (55 s). The test stops Node after 600 s and names the first request without a response with `Node was stopped at its limit of 600 s`; checked here with the limit set to 20 s and a stand-in that hangs after three requests. Declined: a failure artifact of the frame files and the module. The failure lines hold both values at each difference, the request frames follow from the tree and the module from the pinned Emscripten, and the native responses, the only part that depends on the runner, are in neither file.

**ENG-2. The engine's web-column budgets have no benchmark**
- **Spec:** engine.md 5 (line 806).
- **Spec first:** small. Say where the harness lives and how it runs.
- **Work:**
  - A Playwright page or worker driver that sends each web-column row to `ui/public/core/partsmith_core.mjs` and compares medians to the budgets, mirroring `Report` and `median` in `partsmith-engine/benches/budgets.rs`.
  - Include wasm memory under 4 GB with a 512 MiB `cache_budget`.
- **Impact:** medium. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** phase 4 (the `apply_fix`, `list_uses` and ghost rows), and B-11's helpers.
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0043, accepted). engine.md 5 now says where the harness lives and how it runs: `ui/e2e/engine-budgets.spec.ts`, one `@perf` test per web row (test 155, 17 rows), run by hand with `PARTSMITH_PERF=1 pnpm -C ui e2e engine-budgets.spec.ts` and never in CI, drives a module worker on a test page (`ui/e2e/pages/engine.html`, `engine-worker.ts`, served as P-12's page) that times the app's `callCore` around `ps_call` on new instances of the shipped module, compiled once. The rows of `benches/budgets.rs` are ported to `ui/e2e/support/engine-budgets.ts` under the same names and kept in step by hand; each reports its median of 7 as a `perf` annotation through one helper shared with `perf.spec.ts` (ui.md 6.7). Tests 156 and 157 check the driver on every e2e run. No project is added to `playwright.config.ts`. `bare_numbers` has no web row, since only `Session::count_bare_numbers` times the count alone (open issue 20). A prototype of the driver here (Chromium 141) read the cold `open_document` of the reference part at a median of 2,339 ms (3 s) and 29,097,984 bytes of wasm memory for the 300-box document (open issue 19). That row stays far from `cache_budget`, so it cannot check ENG-3's bound; ENG-3's "Depends on" line says so.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done, on `main` since wave B (cae269a). The driver is `ui/e2e/pages/engine.html` with its module worker `engine-worker.ts` (`start`, `call`, `memoryBytes`, `end` on `partsmithEngine`; the module compiled once and each instance made through Emscripten's `instantiateWasm` synchronously, so a failed instantiation rejects `start` instead of leaving it waiting), `ui/e2e/support/engine-budgets.ts` holds `openEngine`, `ok` and the 17 rows of `benches/budgets.rs` ported under their names (`MEMORY_ROW`, `TIMED_ROWS`), and `expectWithinBudgets` moved to `ui/e2e/support/perf.ts`, which `perf.spec.ts` imports. Tests 156 and 157 and the unit test of `ok` pass, as does the full Chromium e2e run (207 tests); two runs of test 155 here wrote all 17 annotations, with `list_elements` of a new state (26 to 27 ms, 25 ms) and `analyze_print` (3.1 s, 900 ms) over their web budgets (engine.md open issue 19). `ui/e2e/README.md`, `ui/AGENTS.md`, `crates/partsmith-engine/AGENTS.md` and the doc comment of `benches/budgets.rs` name the bench and the rule that keeps its rows in step with the desktop's. Only a run on the reference machine decides the budgets.
- **Status (2026-10-27, review):** done; review findings 1 to 3 fixed. The warm-up covers only the code that opening the reference part runs: engine.md section 5, ADR 0043 (decision 1 and the Warm-up option, which now also weighs a warm-up per row) and the spec's comment say so, and that code only one row runs is optimized during that row's first samples, which the median of 7 absorbs; open issue 19 records the first samples measured here (`export_python` 13.08 ms then 5.42 to 8.77 ms, the first pick of a new state 3.37 ms then 0.23 to 0.30 ms, while row 3's cold opens show no slow first sample). The engine page marks its worker's `error` event (`data-error`, the `ErrorEvent`'s message or `its script did not load`), and `openEngine` rejects with `The engine page's worker did not start: {error}` instead of timing out after 15 s; test 157 gained cases (c), a worker script that throws, and (d), a worker script answered by a 404, which failed with the bare timeout before the fix. The rows' `beforeAll` calls `warmUp(engine)` of the support module, so the warm-up and the rows read the reference part from one place, and the spec file takes `Answer` from `pages/engine-worker.ts`; it keeps its own `SAMPLES`, the spec's 7, on purpose.

**ENG-3. `cache_budget` does not bound the total cache size**
- **Spec:** engine.md 3.17, 5.
- **Spec first:** yes. Either:
  - (a) state the M1 approximation in engine.md 3.17, section 2, `options.rs` and an open issue; or
  - (b) split the budget between `Recompute::with_cache_limit` and `DisplayCache::new` in `Session::new`, with a unit test.
- **Impact:** low. Today the bound is about twice the budget, which on wasm is 512 MiB of display data plus 2048 results. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** nothing. ENG-2's memory row cannot check it (engine.md open issue 19: 300 boxes hold 29 MB); the check needs a document that fills the caches, which ENG-3 leaves to engine.md open issue 21.
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0044, accepted). Option (b): engine.md 3.17 has `Session::new` give the tessellation cache `cache_budget / 2` bytes and recompute the other half as a limit of results at 256 KiB each (2048 at 1 GiB, 1024 at 512 MiB), section 2's doc comment of `cache_budget` says so, the split is `cache_shares` beside `ESTIMATED_RESULT_BYTES` in `session.rs`, and test 158 checks it in unit tests of `session.rs` and `display.rs`. No document fills the caches to the budget: that needs gigabytes natively and a measure of a result's size, which recompute does not have, so test 158 checks each cache's share at small budgets and open issue 21 records the estimate with the two ways to a bound in bytes.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done. `session.rs`'s `cache_shares` makes the split and `Session::new` gives its halves to `DisplayCache::new` and `Recompute::with_cache_limit`; the doc comments of `cache_budget` and `ESTIMATED_RESULT_BYTES` say so, and `partsmith-recompute` is unchanged. Test 158 passes (six unit tests in `session.rs` and `display.rs`). The budgets bench, run here before (cae269a) and after in alternating rounds, shows no row slower: every median is within the run-to-run spread, and the two rows that read slower in the first three rounds (STL export and `apply_fix`) read the same in five more (STL 24.1 ms before and 23.6 ms after, `apply_fix` 75.0 ms and 75.1 ms), since no row's session comes near either half.

- **Spec:** engine.md 3.15 (line 492); protocol.md line 1394.

- **Work:** run the dispatch panic test, or a test-only panic trigger built with the core's link arguments, on `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` in CI.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** B-1, and the shared job with ENG-1.

No test-only trigger in the module: it would need a fourth export or a test-only command.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done, on `main` since wave B (cae269a). No production code changed. `ci.yml`'s `kernel-wasm` runs `cargo test -p partsmith-engine --lib --target wasm32-unknown-emscripten dispatch::` in a step of its own, after the sketch suite (delivery.md 2.4, test 66); here its 11 dispatch tests pass under Node, the two that protocol.md test 84 names among them, and `tests/build_script.rs` checks the `wasm-release` profile and `scripts/build-wasm.sh`. Only CI shows the step's cold build time on the runner.
- **Status (2026-10-27, review):** done; no defect found in this item. Run again here: the 11 dispatch tests pass under Node, the two of protocol.md test 84 among them. delivery.md 2.4's `kernel-wasm` row no longer calls its first step "unchanged" (review finding 4).

**ENG-5. `display_failed` has no spec, and `duplicate_element_name` names the body by id**
- **Spec:** engine.md 3.4 and 3.8; docs/guidelines/errors.md.
- **Spec first:** yes.
- **Work:**
  - Specify `display_failed` with a compliant sentence that names the body. The kernel text moves to `Diagnostic.detail`. Add an exact-text test.
  - Delete the paragraph and the dead check for `duplicate_element_name`, or keep it only as a `debug_assert`. Naming already makes names unique (naming.md line 571).
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** phase 1 PR1 (`Diagnostic.detail`) and the phase 3 message lint.
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0044). engine.md 3.8 specifies `display_failed`: a `warning`, first among the messages of the feature that produced the body state, `Body '{name}' cannot be shown, and Partsmith could not tell why. Report this with the details.` (a STEP solid kept invalid can cause it too, so it does not call itself a bug), with `detail` `body {id}: {text}` from the failure's technical text, and the status raised to `warning`; 3.4 sends `recomputed` again when these messages change without a recompute (after `set_tessellation`); test 159 states the exact text and the whole `Diagnostic` in `src/events.rs`'s unit tests, since no document a feature builds makes a display fail. The `duplicate_element_name` paragraph is replaced by a sentence that names naming's own checks (naming.md 3.3 step 7, 2.6 and 2.7), and the check goes without a `debug_assert`: a duplicate already fails the element set, so it would show as `display_failed`. The engine's tests do not use `partsmith-message-lint` (a new dev-dependency of the engine); the text has no id, code, backtick or kernel text, which the lint would catch.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done. `display.rs` gives a table or mesh that cannot be built an internal error whose detail is the technical text (`KernelError::detail`, `FeatureError::detail`, or the engine's own sentence), `events.rs` builds the `display_failed` warning from it and has `Emitted` send `recomputed` again when a layer's failures change without a recompute, and `status.rs` puts the failures first among the messages of the feature that provides the body state and raises `ok` to `warning`; the duplicate name check is gone, and element table entries sort by name alone. Test 159 passes (nine unit tests in `events.rs`, which replace four that checked the old `error`). Review fix: a failure keeps the body, its provider and the technical text, and `status.rs` words the message with the body's current name each time statuses are made, since a rename hits the cache and does not try the display again (engine.md 3.8 says so and test 159 (f) checks it); a failure of a body no longer in the document reports nothing.

**ENG-6. No byte-for-byte golden 3MF**
- **Spec:** technical design 17; io.md 3.9.
