  - Regeneration is opt-in and refused in CI, like `bless_mode`. Document it in the engine's AGENTS.md.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-27, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0044). engine.md 3.20 describes `box.3mf` and `two-bodies.3mf`, pinned on CI's `rust` build (ubuntu-24.04, OCCT built by `scripts/build-occt.sh` with that image's compiler and C library, which the reference container matches; test 160 runs on x86_64 Linux with glibc only), the five changes after which a regenerated golden is expected (zip or its deflate backend or level; an OCCT bump or a change of the tessellation or kernel build flags; the compiler or C library that builds or runs OCCT; how the fixtures' box and cylinder are built; an intended change of the package or its inputs), and regeneration only with `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-engine --test integration export_and_import::t160`, refused under `CI` with the examples' message; delivery.md 2.8, its README list and its error table name it. Tests 160 (one per golden, ignored on every other target) and 161 (the bless rule, as delivery.md test 25) are appended after test 159, since new tests take the next ids; 3.13 step 7, open issue 18 and io.md 3.9 name the goldens.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done here; the first CI run confirms the bytes. `box.3mf` (1,123 bytes) and `two-bodies.3mf` (6,406 bytes) were blessed in the reference container (Ubuntu 24.04.5, GCC 13.3, glibc 2.39, OCCT 7.9.3 from `/opt/occt/7.9.3/native`), tests 160 and 161 pass, and the engine's AGENTS.md has the command, its filter and the pinned build. Until CI's `rust` job passes test 160 with them they count as blessed outside CI (engine.md 3.20); on Windows and macOS (`platforms.yml`) test 160 shows as ignored. Review fix: `golden_outcome` decides test 160 without reading a file, and four more `t160_` tests, run on every target, check its outcomes (equal, bless with its `blessed` line, and the whole missing and differing texts).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done; CI confirmed the bytes. CI's `rust` job runs test 160 through `cargo test --workspace` on ubuntu-24.04 and has passed on every push to `main` since the goldens reached it, six runs from 40632129142 (470c0ed, 2026-10-28) to 41103065188 (228ad1d), so they no longer count as blessed outside CI (engine.md 3.20).

**ENG-7. A STEP solid without volume makes measure report a bug** (added on 2026-10-28 by the spec of IMP-1)
- **Spec:** engine.md 3.11 (measure); kernel.md 3.8.1 read item 3; docs/guidelines/errors.md (Tone).
- **Problem:** a STEP solid whose void is its own outer shell has no volume, stays invalid after repair and builds through the import feature with its `import_warning`. `partsmith rebuild` of a document importing it then fails with `internal_error`: `Session::measure` (`crates/partsmith-engine/src/inspect.rs:155`) asks the kernel for the body's center of mass and maps the kernel's error to `EngineError::unexpected`, which presents a user's file as a Partsmith bug.
- **Spec first:** yes, one of: measure reports a body without volume as a user condition (no center of mass, with a message that names the body), or `read_step` leaves such solids out and counts them (kernel.md 3.8.1 and ADR 0045's counts).
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** IMP-1.
- **Status (2026-10-30, spec):** spec text written for the first option, no code yet. Each path that asks the kernel for a volume-dependent property of a body was run here with `SELF_VOID` (engine.md test 162: the STEP export of `box.ptsm` with its outer shell listed as its void too, which reads back as one solid still invalid after repair, with volume 0 up to rounding, area 5200 and 6 faces; the same edit of features/import.md's `BYTES`, and of kernel.md test 140's `HOLLOW` with its void replaced by its outer shell, behaves the same, while `HOLLOW` with its outer shell as a second void reads back with volume 2496 and a center of mass). Only the body target of `measure` failed, with `internal_error` (the kernel's `center_of_mass` gives `InvalidInput`), and through it `partsmith rebuild` (exit 1, also with `--json` and `--checks`) and Python's `Part.volume()`, `area()`, `center_of_mass()` and `bounding_box()`; by reading `ui/src/inspector/measurements.ts`, the UI's Volume row stays empty, since its volume meter logs the error at info level. Element measures, `list_elements`, display meshes, `analyze_print` (health `ok`), STL, 3MF, OBJ and STEP export, `export_python`, `validate --rebuild` and `export` of the CLI worked. The kernel's no-volume error holds only below an absolute 1e-12 mm³, and the rounding left by a shell and its void grows with the body: the same edit of `fillet.ptsm` (1.8e-12 mm³), of a 213.7 x 177.1 x 93.3 mm box (1.2e-10 mm³) and of a cylinder of radius 137.3 mm and height 191.7 mm (2.3e-10 mm³) measured today without an error and with a center of mass that means nothing, also in `rebuild --json` and Python. So engine.md section 3.11 calls a body without volume one whose volume is at most 1e-9 times its area to the power 3/2 (`has_volume` of `src/inspect.rs`), measures it without asking the kernel for a center of mass, with no error and no message of its own (the import's `import_warning` is the root cause), and keeps every kernel or feature failure an `internal_error` as before; tests 162 (`SELF_VOID` and `SELF_VOID_FILLET`) to 164 (the limit), and open issue 22 for what stays (print analysis and export see the box its faces bound, and later features word their volume checks untruthfully on it, none an `internal_error`). kernel.md section 3.6 item 1 says the kernel's center of mass of such a shape means nothing above its limit, section 3.8.1 read item 3 that a kept solid can have no volume, and open issue 15 that the second option waits for ADR 0045's review; the kernel does not change. cli.md section 3.3 leaves `center_of_mass` out of such a body's JSON (test 65, with `store_self_void_asset` of `tests/common`); python-api.md section 3.3 has `center_of_mass()` raise `PartsmithError` with `Part.center_of_mass(): Body '<name>' has no volume, so it has no center of mass.`, and section 3.2 has `Document.import_step` warn about the problems the import starts, as `Param.set` does, so a Python user sees the import's warning that says why (test 116). No protocol or file-format schema (`Measurement` keeps its doc comments), UI code or example changes, so no ADR.
- **Status (2026-10-30, implemented):** done. `Session::measure` (`crates/partsmith-engine/src/inspect.rs`) reads the body's volume and area first and asks the kernel for the center of mass only when `has_volume(volume, area)` holds (|volume| above 1e-9 times area^(3/2), `NO_VOLUME_RATIO`), so a body without volume is measured without `center_of_mass` and every kernel failure stays `internal_error`. The CLI's `BodyMetrics::center_of_mass` is an `Option` left out of the JSON when absent (`commands/rebuild.rs`); Python's `Part.center_of_mass()` raises `PartsmithError` with python-api.md section 3.3's message when the measurement has none, and `Document.import_step` sends its command through `_command_and_warn` (which now passes request buffers), so it warns about the problems it starts (`python/partsmith/document.py`). No kernel, protocol, file-format, UI or example change, so no schema, ADR or blessed file changed; the AGENTS.md of `partsmith-engine` and `partsmith-py` name the new behavior, and the CLI tests' `store_box_step_asset` and `store_self_void_asset` share one `store_step_asset`. A container restart stopped the first implementation run during its Chromium e2e run; the second run checked the code against the spec and ran every check again: engine.md tests 162 to 164 with the whole engine suite, cli.md test 65 with the whole CLI suite (the examples included) and the rebuild unit tests, python-api.md test 116 and the import unit test with the whole pytest run, the Python binding, wasm (native) and desktop suites, the schema check, clippy and fmt, the UI lint, typecheck, Vitest and script tests, and the Chromium e2e run on the web core rebuilt after the engine change. Windows, macOS, Firefox and WebKit stay with CI; the change is platform-independent Rust and Python.
- **Status (2026-10-30, review):** done; review findings 1 and 2 fixed. Finding 1: a body with volume but less than 1e-12 mm³ (a `box` of 0.00005 mm sides, 1.25e-13 mm³, down to the smallest box of `LINEAR_TOLERANCE` sides) made `measure` fail with `internal_error`, and with it `partsmith rebuild` and Python's `Part.volume()`, since the kernel's `center_of_mass` failed below that absolute limit although `has_volume` held; this predates ENG-7. kernel.md section 3.6 item 1 now fails only below 1e-20 mm³ or on a volume that is not finite, the limit below which OCCT's `GProp_GProps::Add` keeps no centroid (`kSmallestCenteredVolume` in `cpp/measure.cpp`), with kernel.md test 143 (`primitives_and_queries.rs`, run natively and under Node) and engine.md test 165 (`measure_and_print.rs`, the 0.00005 mm and 1e-6 mm cubes), both of which fail with the old limit; engine.md section 3.11 says a small body is not a body without volume. Finding 2: engine.md open issue 22 now also lists the mirror of an imported solid still invalid after repair (code `kernel`, the `transform` hint `Report it with the details.`, checked on `SELF_VOID` and on a solid made as `VOID_TWICE` is) and the linear pattern that turns `SELF_VOID` into three full boxes with status `ok`; rewording them belongs to features.md section 4 or kernel.md section 4.3. No protocol, file-format, UI or example change, so no schema, ADR or blessed file changed. The web core was rebuilt after the kernel change for the Chromium e2e run.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done, with no human gate: ENG-7 changes no schema, public API, license, dependency or capability. The implemented line's statements that the kernel did not change and that the change is platform-independent Rust and Python stopped holding with the review, which changed the kernel (`kSmallestCenteredVolume`, 1e-20 mm³, in `crates/partsmith-occt-sys/cpp/measure.cpp`). No CI or platforms run has seen it yet: CI runs kernel.md test 143 natively and under Node once wave D is on `main`, and the next platforms run after that is the first to run test 143 and engine.md tests 162 to 165 on `windows-2025` and `macos-15` (`rust-platforms` tests partsmith-occt-sys and partsmith-engine). What engine.md open issue 22 keeps is not this item's work, and no item of this list holds it: the mirror of an imported solid still invalid after repair, with or without volume, fails with code `kernel` and `Report it with the details.`, which words a user's file as a fault of Partsmith, the kind of defect ENG-7 was opened for, and a linear pattern turns a body without volume into full boxes with status `ok`; rewording them is a change of features.md section 4 or kernel.md section 4.3. ENG-7's other option, leaving such solids out of the import, waits for ADR 0045's review (IMP-1, kernel.md open issue 15).
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** signed off: the owner accepted the kernel's limit as built (`kSmallestCenteredVolume`, 1e-12 to 1e-20 mm³, kernel.md test 143). The platforms runs 41456376800 (6901359, 2026-11-02) and 41751363019 (2e4b9ac, 2026-11-04, in attempt 2, after a rerun of its failed `firefox` job, ui.md open issue 41) passed. They were the first two to run test 143 and engine.md tests 162 to 165 on `windows-2025` and `macos-15`.

**ENG-8. Copies of an imported solid that stays invalid after repair** (added on 2026-11-03 for engine.md open issue 22)
- **Spec:** engine.md open issue 22; kernel.md sections 2.13, 3.5 and 4.3; features.md sections 2.7, 2.8, 3.5 and 4.1; features/mirror.md, linear_pattern.md and circular_pattern.md section 3.3; docs/guidelines/errors.md (Tone).
- **Problem:** `mirror(Plane.YZ)` and `mirror(Plane.XY)` of an imported solid that stays invalid after repair, without volume (`SELF_VOID`) or with it (`VOID_TWICE`), fail with code `kernel` and `mirror(): Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build this mirror. Report it with the details.` (the `transform` hint of kernel.md section 4.3), which words a user's file as a fault of Partsmith. And `linear_pattern(Axis.X, 3, 30)` of the body without volume builds with status `ok` and turns it into three full boxes (72000 mm³). A body with no volume gains material with no message.
- **Spec first:** yes. How the kernel tells a result that is invalid because its input already was from a fault of Partsmith, and one rule for the copy features on a body that is not valid or has no volume, with their acceptance tests.
- **Human gate:** a public API change of `partsmith-occt-sys` (`KernelErrorKind::InvalidInputShape`) and of `partsmith-features` (`FeatureError::BodyNotValid`, `has_volume`), ADR 0053.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** nothing (ENG-7 and IMP-1 are on `main`).
- **Status (2026-11-03, spec):** spec text and tests written, no code yet (ADR 0053, proposed, needs human review, with the options still open). Every feature that copies, moves, combines or splits a body was run first through recompute on features/import.md's `BYTES` made into `SELF_VOID` and on `VOID_TWICE`, and the kernel calls behind them on both solids. Every mirror and scale of either failed in the kernel's whole-result check (`InvalidResult`, hint `Report it with the details.`). A rigid move of either is not checked. The patterns of `SELF_VOID` gained material (three copies 30 mm apart along X: three full blocks, 18000 mm³, status ok), warned `no_effect` untruly (copies that overlap, a circular pattern of 4), or left a new body with a shape that holds no solid (a circular pattern into it, status ok). The booleans, the joins and `split_body` gave results from right to wrong with no kernel error, and joining the plate of features/import.md test 7 with `SELF_VOID` as the tool left the plate with no solid, status ok (features.md open issue 23). A fillet of `VOID_TWICE` and a shell of `SELF_VOID` fail with `InvalidResult`, whose hints do not blame Partsmith. Kernel: section 3.5 checks the shape a mirror or scale `transform` was given, as the caller passed it, only after repair of the copy has failed. It raises `Partsmith_InvalidInputShape` (kind `InvalidInputShape`) when that shape was already invalid, with a hint that names the STEP file (section 4.3, tests 144 and 145, open issue 16). Every other operation keeps `InvalidResult`. Features: section 3.5 makes mirror, linear pattern and circular pattern read a source whose shape holds no solid as `BodyEmpty`. Section 3.5 also makes them refuse a source body without volume (`has_volume`, which moves from the engine to partsmith-features, section 2.7, with engine.md test 164's cases in features.md test 19) or a mirror whose `transform` fails with `InvalidInputShape`, with `body_not_valid` (section 4.1): `Body '{body}' holds a solid from '{feature}' that is not valid, so {this} cannot copy it. Repair the STEP file, or export it again from the program that made it.` when the solid came from an import, with Show of that feature. The feature is `made_by`, the newest writer of the body whose state before it is empty or valid, found by the copy feature on its failure path. Recompute and the protocol do not change. They fail rather than build with a warning: the least code, and every body stays as it was. Tests: kernel.md 144 (`tests/exchange.rs`, with `self_void` beside `void_listed_twice`) and 145 (`tests/values.rs`), with unit tests of the kind in `src/error.rs`; features.md test 19 of section 6.5 (`tests/body_not_valid.rs` of partsmith-features) and unit tests of `Source` in `builtin/copy.rs`; features/mirror.md 14 to 16, features/linear_pattern.md 25 and features/circular_pattern.md 19 (`feature_<type>.rs` of partsmith-recompute, with the STEP edit `self_void` added to `tests/feature_support/import.rs` beside `void_listed_twice`, as the engine's and the CLI's tests make it); features/import.md's fixture `SELF_VOID` and test 17 (`feature_import.rs`, whose `HOLLOW` and `VOID_TWICE` builders moved to `tests/feature_support/import.rs` for the copy features' tests); python-api.md test 117 (`tests/test_documents.py`), in the document of its test 116. engine.md open issue 22 is resolved. features.md open issue 23 keeps the other features with their options. No schema, protocol, file-format, UI or example change, and ADR 0045's counts do not change. The implementer changes `cpp/validity.cpp` and `cpp/transforms.cpp` (`ensure_valid` checks the transform's input when repair fails), `cpp/shim.h` and `src/error.rs` of partsmith-occt-sys; `builtin/copy.rs` (`Source::new` and `Source::copy`), the error and message modules and `has_volume` of partsmith-features; and `src/inspect.rs` of partsmith-engine, which imports `has_volume` and drops its own test 164 cases; then rebuilds the web core. kernel.md test 144 runs natively and under Node in CI, and on Windows and macOS with the next platforms run.
- **Status (2026-11-03, implemented):** done, and waiting for its human gate (ADR 0053). Kernel: a mirror or scale `transform` (`cpp/transforms.cpp`) now goes through `ensure_valid_copy` of `cpp/validity.cpp`, which shares its repair step (`repair`) with `ensure_valid`. It checks the transform's input with `BRepCheck_Analyzer` only when the repaired copy stays invalid, and raises `Partsmith_InvalidInputShape` (`cpp/shim.h`, `raise_invalid_input_shape` in `cpp/shape.cpp`) when that input was not valid either. `src/error.rs` maps the exception to `KernelErrorKind::InvalidInputShape`, with the detail name `invalid_input_shape` and the hint of kernel.md section 4.3. Every other operation keeps `InvalidResult`. Features: `has_volume` moved from the engine's `src/inspect.rs` to `src/output.rs` of partsmith-features (exported at the crate root), and the engine imports it. `Source::new` (`builtin/copy.rs`) reads a shape without a solid as `BodyEmpty` and a source without volume as `BodyNotValid`, with the area summed from the face fingerprints of the body's topology. `Source::copy` turns a kernel `InvalidInputShape` into `BodyNotValid` with that error as its `cause`. `made_by` walks the writer chain from the newest writer back and stops at the first state that is empty or valid. A chain without a writer is `Internal`. `FeatureError::BodyNotValid` (`src/error/mod.rs`) has code `body_not_valid`, the two texts of features.md section 4.1 (the one that names the feature when the record of `made_by` has type `import`), Show of `made_by`, its cause's detail and its cause as `source`. The only test edit is the removal of the engine's own test 164 unit tests, which went with the function (features.md test 19 holds those cases now, as engine.md test 164 says). No schema, protocol, file-format, UI or example changed, and ADR 0045's counts did not change. Checks run here: fmt, clippy, the partsmith-occt-sys, partsmith-features, partsmith-recompute, partsmith-engine, partsmith-cli (examples included), partsmith-py, partsmith-wasm and partsmith-desktop suites, the schema check, pytest, and the Chromium e2e run on the rebuilt web core. CI runs kernel.md test 144 under Node. The next platforms run is the first to run it on Windows and macOS.
- **Status (2026-11-03, review):** review findings 1 to 5 fixed; the item still waits for its human gate (ADR 0053). Finding 1: the volume test read the whole body. An import puts every solid of a STEP file in one body. With `TWO_SELF_VOID` (features/import.md's `TWO` with its cube `A` edited as `SELF_VOID`), `LP(DIR(X, "3", "20"))` built with status ok and no message and gained 1000 mm³. A circular pattern of 4 built with status ok. `Source::new` now tests each solid of a body of several, with one kernel `volume` per solid and the areas of its faces in the topology (`holds_solid_without_volume`, `Shape::locate`), and a body of one solid as before (features.md section 3.5; linear_pattern.md test 26, circular_pattern.md test 20 and mirror.md test 14, with the fixture's own test in `feature_import.rs` and unit tests in `builtin/copy.rs`). On bench50 the three copy features whose source holds several solids measure about 4.6 ms of volumes in a release build. Finding 2: `made_by` tested states with `Shape::is_valid` while the source was refused by volume, and named a move after the import when the import's solid was valid without volume. It now uses the test the source failed: `Shape::is_valid` after a kernel `InvalidInputShape`, else the volume test, with a unit test for each. Finding 3: `made_by` walked from the newest writer back with a full validity check per state. It now walks from the oldest writer and stops at the first result that fails, one test for a body its import made. bench50's body with a `SELF_VOID` lump, imported, moved 20 times and mirrored through XY failed in 10.7 s before and in 0.11 s now (debug build). With a `VOID_TWICE` lump the mirror still takes 3.6 s after 20 moves (0.6 s without moves): `made_by` is 0.14 s of it, and the rest is the kernel's `transform` and input check on a body moved 20 times, which this item does not change. No validity cache on `BodyState` was added. The walk now tests one state in that case. Finding 4: the AGENTS.md sentence of partsmith-features, ADR 0053 decision 4 (which features carry the topology and the volume of their result) and the list of items that wait only for a human, which now holds ENG-8. Finding 5: `Source::new` calls `common::solid_body` and the new `common::body_empty`. The STEP edit of the crate's unit tests moved to `test_support.rs` (`step_box`, `self_void_step`, `imported`). The copies of that edit in the test trees of partsmith-occt-sys, partsmith-recompute, partsmith-engine, partsmith-cli and Python stay. Those crates share no test code. No schema, protocol, file-format, UI or example change, and ADR 0045's counts did not change. Checks run here: fmt, clippy of the workspace, partsmith-features (557 unit and 610 integration tests), partsmith-recompute (106 and 1196), partsmith-engine (263 and 448), the CLI's `examples::` tests (118), `schema --check`, pytest (2367 passed, 1 skipped) and the Chromium e2e run (229 passed) on the web core rebuilt after the change. Each new unit test failed on the code before the fix. The run of bench50 from an empty cache took 2.22 to 2.31 s with the fix and 2.28 s without it (release build, this container), over the 1 s budget of the reference machine both ways. partsmith-occt-sys did not change. The desktop crate was not run.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0053 option (a) as built, with the public API of `KernelErrorKind`, `FeatureError` and `has_volume`. features.md open issue 23 leaves the other features as they are. The platforms run 41751363019 (2e4b9ac, 2026-11-04, in attempt 2, after a rerun of its failed `firefox` job, ui.md open issue 41) passed with it on Windows and macOS.

### ui/ (outside the blockers)

**UI-1. `index.html` lacks the color-scheme meta**
- **Spec:** ui.md 2.16.8 (line 2169).
- **Spec first:** no.
- **Work:** add `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark" />`, and a Vitest test that reads index.html.
- **Impact:** low. Without it, a dark-mode OS sees a light flash at cold start. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** nothing. It can land any time.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done in the UI gap wave of 2026-10-13. `ui/index.html` declares `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark" />` in its `head` (ui.md 2.16.8), and U-163 (`ui/src/theme/color-scheme.test.ts`) reads the file and checks that it holds exactly one such meta, in `head`, with that content; it passes.

**UI-2. The web build ships without a CSP or hardening headers**
- **Spec:** desktop.md 2.8 (line 381) and open issue 5; delivery.md 2.7.
- **Spec first:** yes. delivery.md 2.7: a CSP adapted from desktop (with `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, `worker-src 'self'` and the ADR 0013 font hosts), `frame-ancestors 'none'`, nosniff and Referrer-Policy.
- **Human gate:** security review.
- **Work:**
  - Update `_headers`, the pinned text in `check-dist.mjs` and its tests, and the vite preview headers.
  - Add an E-test that sees no `securitypolicyviolation`.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** B-1 (a preview deploy to check it) and B-9.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done; the security review of ADR 0049 (proposed) is the gate. `ui/public/_headers` holds the five headers of delivery.md 2.7 (409 bytes), `EXPECTED_HEADERS` and the two messages of section 4 in `check-dist.mjs` match them, and the Vite plugin `webHeaders` reads the file when the configuration loads and sends all five from the preview and all but the policy from the dev server (ADR 0049 option (h)), 304s included; the visual tests run with `bypassCSP` (ui.md 6.6). E-161 reads the page's and the core worker's violations from their start through a buffered `ReportingObserver` (`reportedViolations` in `e2e/helpers.ts`), so a refusal during the worker's startup that does not stop the core fails it, and every other E-test imports a `test` from `e2e/helpers.ts` that fails it in Chromium when its page reported a violation (ui.md 6.3); `ui/AGENTS.md` states what the policy refuses. Tests 14, 18 and 65 pass, `pnpm check:dist` passes on a fresh build, and E-161 and the other tests of `pnpm e2e` pass in Chromium under the policy. Not run here: the `@visual` and `@perf` tests under the policy, Firefox and WebKit (the platforms workflow) and the headers as Pages serves them (delivery.md open issue 19, human checks).
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; it waits only for the security review of ADR 0049 decision 1 and the human checks of delivery.md open issues 17 and 19 (the headers as Pages serves them, its 304 responses included). The `@engines` tests passed in Firefox and WebKit against the preview, which sends the policy, in the three platforms runs since the policy reached `main` (41018391709 on 9665323 to 41103071504 on 228ad1d).
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** the security review is done: the owner accepted ADR 0049 decision 1 with option (a), the five headers enforced, as built. The checks of delivery.md open issues 17 and 19 (the headers and 304 responses as Pages serves them, and the policy in Firefox and Safari) stay checks at the gate.

**UI-3. Tab order and target size have no tests**
- **Spec:** ui.md 1.1, 3.24; technical design 13.
- **Spec first:** yes. Two new E-tests in ui.md 6.5:
  - tab through the regions of 3.24 on cube.ptsm and check the order and focus return;
  - every visible button, `[role=button]` and input is at least 24 by 24 CSS px at scale 1.

- **Human gate:** only if the axe dependency is added (with an ADR entry).
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** done without axe. A notification that goes away with focus inside it gives focus to the 3D view (a layout effect of its card, U-238), and a command's inspector that closes after the control that had focus is gone gives it to the 3D view (`returnFocus` in `src/layout/focus.ts`, which the side panel shares, U-239); the browser row's swatch is a `size-6 -m-1` button around a bordered `size-4` square and the Problems panel's feature label is `min-h-6 min-w-6 -my-0.5`, 24 pixels high in the 20 pixels of its line (U-240), so neither row moves and no visual baseline changes for them (a screenshot of the Problems panel on `fillet-error.ptsm` matched the one taken before ADR 0049 byte for byte). E-162 and E-163 pass in Chromium. Tab order on macOS is ui.md open issue 43's human check.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left, and although axe, the only gate named above, was not added, the item waits for a human: the ADR log records ADR 0049 as needing review of the scope of the target size rule (decision 3, the marks ui.md 3.24 leaves out, with options 3(b) to 3(d) and ui.md open issue 42), and ui.md open issue 43 asks the reviewer to record the tab order in Safari 17 and in the macOS desktop app with the system's Keyboard navigation setting on and off. Option 3(b) changes the sketch overlay's glyphs and labels and the tests of `screen.ts`, 3(c) the timeline's rollback marker, and 3(d) adds axe as a dependency with an ADR row; a region the keyboard cannot reach with the setting off is a defect of ui.md 3.24, which then names the keys that reach it. E-162 and E-163 run in Chromium in CI's `e2e` job on every push to `main` since they reached it (the latest in run 41103065188, 228ad1d).
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** signed off for M1: the owner accepted ADR 0049 decision 2 (keyboard) as built and decision 3 with option (a). The sketch's glyphs (18 pixels, stacked 20 apart) and dimension labels stay under WCAG 2.2's 24-pixel minimum and its spacing exception. The owner revisits that after M1 (ui.md open issue 42). The tab order in Safari 17 and the macOS app (ui.md open issue 43) stays a check at the gate.

**UI-4. Two ui.md 5 budget rows have no measurement point**
- **Spec:** ui.md 5, 2.18, 6.7.
- **Spec first:** yes. Add 2.18 names (for example `ps:apply-events`) and P-tests in 6.7 for:
  - applying `document_changed` plus `recomputed` for 50 features, 8 ms;
  - the expression validation round trip, 50 ms.

U-150 to U-152 pass. P-10 and P-11 in `ui/e2e/perf.spec.ts` measure the two budgets of ui.md 5 (8 ms and 50 ms); they run by hand with `PARTSMITH_PERF=1`, passed here in Chromium, and only a run on the reference machine decides them.

**UI-5. The worker transfer budget (2 ms for a 50 MB frame) is not measured**
- **Spec:** protocol.md 5.
- **Spec first:** small. The protocol.md 5 preamble names where it is measured.
- **Work:** a Vitest browser-mode bench next to `frame.bench.ts`, or an `@perf` e2e test. It does a real Worker `postMessage` with a transfer list and asserts the sent buffer is detached afterwards.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** B-11 if it goes the e2e route.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done in the UI gap wave of 2026-10-13, by the e2e route. P-12 (ui.md 6.7) in `ui/e2e/perf.spec.ts` drives `ui/e2e/pages/transfer.html`, whose module worker (`transfer-worker.ts`) posts 20 response frames of 50 MB, one at a time, with the frame in the transfer list; the test checks that every sent buffer is detached and that each frame decodes, and protocol.md 5's preamble names it. It passed here in Chromium; only a run on the reference machine decides the 2 ms budget.

### partsmith-py and python/

**PY-1. `Document.save` replaces the .ptsm before writing its assets**
- **Spec:** python-api.md line 578; engine.md 3.14.
- **Spec first:** yes. The order becomes: assets first, written atomically, replacing any file whose BLAKE3 does not match its name; then the document; then `mark_saved`.
- **Work:** change `document.py:149-163` and add tests in `test_documents.py`.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** schedule the spec text with B-5, so the hosts copy the right rule.
- **Status (2026-10-23):** done; review findings 1 to 8 fixed. file-format.md 3.11 holds the write order for every host to cite (ADR 0036, accepted for Python): the order and the failure rules bind every host, while step 1's entry check (read a regular file of the asset's size, replace any other entry) binds Python and is proposed for the desktop and the web until B-5's capability review, and the web's limits (no link detection, no flush) and the linked `assets/` folder (Python follows it) are stated there. python-api.md 2.10, 3.1 and 3.7 hold the Python rule; tests 97 to 102 (the save) and 103 (export errors) pass. `Document.save` (`python/partsmith/document.py`) writes the assets, then the document, then sends `mark_saved`; `python/partsmith/_files.py` flushes each file with `os.fsync` before its `os.replace`, raises a failed write again under the target's name, and has `holds`, which looks with `os.lstat` and reads a regular file of the asset's size through one descriptor opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK` and checked with `os.path.samestat`, so an entry swapped for a link or a pipe after the look is never read; `python -m partsmith build` names the failed file. python-api.md open issue 10 records that Python replaces a linked document file while the desktop writes through it, and points at ADR 0037's options (A) to (C). The desktop options of ADR 0036 wait for B-5's capability review.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** on `main` since wave A (2a202ac, 2026-10-24).
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** ADR 0036 is accepted for every host, with ADR 0037. For a linked document file the owner chose ADR 0037 option (A): both hosts write through the link to the file it points to. The assets stay next to the link. That is this item's follow-up. Python's `Document.save` and its exports change (python-api.md 3.1, 3.7 and open issue 10). Wave F's python-links track specifies and builds the change.
- **Status (2026-11-04, spec):** python-api.md 3.1 and 3.7 write a document or export file that is a symbolic link through the link. The target is the file `os.path.realpath(path, strict=True)` names. The temporary file goes in its folder. The link stays as it is. A link that leads to no file (a missing file or a loop) fails before a temporary file is created, as the desktop's write does. Every failure names the link as given (3.1, 3.7, section 4). The assets stay next to the link (ADR 0037 decision 6). New tests 118 to 122 cover a linked document saved through its link (its temporary file in the target's folder), a link to a link, failures through a link (a missing file, a loop, a failed flush, a link to a folder), an export to a linked path, and the link staying the same entry (`python -m partsmith build -o` included, and its failure through a link). They skip only on a `NotImplementedError` or on Windows's privilege error (`winerror` 1314, without Developer Mode or the "Create symbolic links" privilege). Any other error fails them. CI runs them on Linux only: no CI job runs pytest on macOS or Windows. Open issue 10 is resolved. file-format.md 3.11 no longer says the hosts differ. desktop.md 3.4 step 4.1 now says that the desktop's write through a link that leads to no file fails before anything is created. Its new test 72 checks it. Found on the way: the CLI's atomic write follows a link too, but `partsmith export --output` and `partsmith schema --write` onto a link that leads to no file replace the link with a regular file. ADR 0037's amendment of 2026-11-04 and cli.md open issue 12 record it as proposed, needs human review, with options (a) fail like the hosts and (b) keep.
- **Status (2026-11-04, built):** `python/partsmith/_files.py` has `write_through_link`, the write of python-api.md 3.1 for a linked file. It looks at the path with `os.path.islink` (an `os.lstat`). For a link it writes to the file `os.path.realpath(path, strict=True)` names, with the temporary file in that file's folder. `write_atomic` and `write_through_link` share one private write (`_replace`). Both report a failure under the path as given. `Document.save` writes the document with `write_through_link` and its assets with `write_atomic`. The export functions write every file with `write_through_link`. `python -m partsmith build` needed no change: its error line already prints the error's file name. That name is now the link. Tests 118 to 122 and the 20 cases of `python/tests/test_unit_links.py` pass on Linux. macOS's `ELOOP`, Windows's errors from `realpath` and the privilege skip ran nowhere (no CI job runs pytest on macOS or Windows).
- **Status (2026-11-04, review):** the write through a link now has the operating system look at the file first. `os.path.realpath` reads each link itself. Linux's `fs.protected_symlinks` never applied to it. An export to a link another user owns in `/tmp` could replace a file of the saver's own, such as `~/.bashrc`. `_link_target` calls `os.stat(path)` first. That call raises `PermissionError` there. `_link_target` fails with `ESTALE` when `realpath` names another file than that look reached (python-api.md 3.1, tests 120 (e) and (f)). The document and export writes keep the permission bits of the file they replace on Linux and macOS (`os.fchmod` before any byte is written), as the desktop and the CLI do (python-api.md 3.1, test 123). Python still replaces a read-only file where the desktop refuses one: python-api.md open issue 12 and ADR 0037's second amendment of 2026-11-04 record that, proposed, needs human review, with options (a) refuse like the desktop (recommended) and (b) keep. The desktop and the CLI resolve links in user space too (desktop.md open issue 14, cli.md open issue 13). Fixing them waits for their tracks. Every Python test now makes its links with the `symbolic_link` helper. A unit test checks that. Tests 118 to 123 and `python/tests/test_unit_links.py` pass on Linux.
- **Follow-up (ADR 0037 option (A), accepted by the owner on 2026-11-04; wave F's python-links track):** done on 2026-11-04. See the built and review status lines above (tests 118 to 123).
- **Status (2026-11-05, decisions):** option (a) of both amendments of ADR 0037. The CLI's `export --output` and `schema --write` fail with exit 3 onto a link whose target is missing and keep the link (cli.md open issue 12). Python refuses a read-only document or export file with `PermissionError` naming the path (python-api.md open issue 12). By the owner's rule of 2026-11-05 the CLI refuses every read-only file it would replace too (the document `fmt` rewrites, an export file and a schema file of `schema --write`), as the desktop and Python do (ADR 0037, Accepted (owner's rule of 2026-11-05)). Wave G's link-writes track builds the three.
- **Status (2026-11-05, spec):** cli.md section 3.7 holds the one write that `fmt`, `export` and `schema --write` share (`files::write`). A link that leads to no file fails before anything is created with `it is a symbolic link to a file that does not exist. Create that file first, or remove the link.` The link stays (tests 66 and 67). A read-only regular file fails with `the file is read-only. Make it writable first.` (test 69). A link is looked through with `std::fs::metadata` first. On Linux and macOS `canonicalize` must name the file that look reached (test 70). A link that leads to a file is still written through (test 68). cli.md open issues 12 and 13 are resolved. New open issue 14 records the read-only refusal, closed. python-api.md 3.1 refuses a read-only document or export file with `PermissionError` (`EACCES`) naming the path as given, before the temporary file is created, on every OS. A document is refused after its assets are written. A read-only asset that holds other bytes is still replaced (test 124; test 123 (d) now points at 124 (b); open issue 12 resolved). desktop.md 3.4 step 4.1 gives the desktop the same look through a link (`link_target`, test 73; open issue 14 resolved). ADR 0037's amendment of 2026-11-05 records four choices of the owner's rule: plain words for the CLI's two refusals, the document's refusal after its assets, Python's check on Windows too, and the look through a link on the desktop and the CLI. No schema, protocol, capability, license or dependency changes. CI runs the CLI's and Python's new tests on Linux only, and the desktop's test 73 on Linux and macOS. Python's check on Windows is checked by no run.
- **Status (2026-11-05, built):** the three writes are built as specified. The CLI's `files::write` (`crates/partsmith-cli/src/files.rs`) is cli.md section 3.7: `link_target` looks through a link with `fs::metadata` and, on Linux and macOS, compares the device and inode of the file `canonicalize` names; `kept_permissions` refuses a read-only regular file; `create_temporary` makes the temporary file with `tempfile::Builder::make_in` and its own `OpenOptions`, whose errors carry no path. The desktop's write calls the same `link_target` (desktop.md 3.4 step 4.1) in place of `canonicalize` alone. Python's `write_through_link` refuses a regular file with no write bit set with `PermissionError` (`EACCES`) before the temporary file is created (`_writable_mode` in `python/partsmith/_files.py`). No test changed in this step. On Windows the CLI's temporary file is no longer marked `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_TEMPORARY` while it is written. The files the CLI writes there are the same as before: tempfile's `persist` sets `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` before the rename. Checked here: `cargo test -p partsmith-cli` (tests 66 to 71 among them), `cargo test -p partsmith-desktop` (test 73), `pytest python/tests` (tests 123 and 124), workspace clippy and `cargo fmt`. CI only: test 73 on macOS. No run checks the Windows paths (the CLI's and the desktop's write without the comparison, and Python's check).
- **Status (2026-11-05, review):** the review found no correctness, security or spec defect. It found three small slips and two nits, all fixed. The built line above said the CLI's files on Windows used to keep `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_TEMPORARY`. They never did: tempfile 3.27.0's `persist` sets `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` before the rename. The line now says so. `LINK_TO_NO_FILE` in `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/writes.rs` is used only by the Unix-only `links` module. A Windows build of the integration tests would warn that it is never used. It now sits in that module. `test_a_file_only_its_owner_may_read_is_refused` in `python/tests/test_unit_links.py` asserted mode `0o400` after the refusal. On Windows `os.chmod` only sets the read-only attribute, and `os.stat` reports `0o444`. The test now compares the mode after the refusal with the mode before it. `_writable_mode`'s docstring now says that a rename would replace a read-only file on Linux and macOS, and that on Windows `os.replace` refuses it after the temporary file is written. `create_temporary`'s doc comment wraps at 100 columns like the rest of the crate. Cleanup: the two copies of the refusal's assertion in `test_documents.py` and `test_unit_links.py` are one helper, `assert_read_only_refusal` in `python/tests/support.py`. The unit tests now check the refusal's text too. Checked here: `cargo fmt`, workspace clippy, `cargo test -p partsmith-cli` (199 unit and 259 integration tests), `cargo test -p partsmith-desktop` (test 73 among them), and `pytest python/tests` (2421 passed, 1 skipped). A local build with the Unix-only code of the CLI's and the desktop's write switched off (a stand-in for Windows) checks both crates' tests with no warning. No run here checks the Windows paths themselves.

**PY-2. Three GUI options have no dedicated Python keyword**
- **Spec:** technical design 10 (line 350); python-api.md 2.7 and 3.5 (line 623); features/extrude.md 98, chamfer.md 80, linear_pattern.md 91; features.md test 23.
- **Spec first:** yes, or an ADR that keeps the generic `feature()` form on purpose.
- **Human gate:** public API.
- **Work:**
  - extrude: two-sided distances. chamfer: `second_distance` and `flip`. linear_pattern: `reverse`, including inside `second`.
  - Emit them from the hooks at `extrude.rs:169`, `chamfer.rs:232` and `linear_pattern.rs:221`.
  - Regenerate the affected twins.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** phase 1 step 8 (its Python changes).
- **Status (2026-10-28, spec):** spec text written, no code yet ([ADR 0048](../adr/0048-python-keywords-gui-round-trip-and-python-budgets.md) decision 1, Proposed, needs human review). Checked against the repository: none of the three options had a keyword (`python/partsmith/features.py`), and the three hooks returned `None` for them, so 8 twins held a generic call (`hole/deleted-point`, whose point is gone, is the ninth and keeps it). The keywords are the fields' labels in snake case: `extrude(..., opposite_distance=)`, `chamfer(..., second_distance=, flip=)`, `linear_pattern(..., reverse=, second_reverse=)`; the owner can still pick `second=(axis, count, spacing, True)`, the generic form on purpose, or other names (the ADR's options (B) to (D)). python-api.md 2.7, 3.5 and 4, tests 82 and 107 to 113; the Python sections of features/extrude.md, chamfer.md and linear_pattern.md; features.md test 23 (now an `import` record). The twins are regenerated by the Python bless through delivery.md 2.8's new twin check 4 (delivery.md test 72). The three hook unit tests that pinned `None` become tests of the new forms, and `crates/partsmith-py/AGENTS.md`'s `features.py` bullet notes the keywords as proposed under ADR 0048.
- **Status (2026-10-28, implemented):** done here, waiting for the human gate of ADR 0048 decision 1 (proposed, needs human review). `python/partsmith/features.py`: `extrude(..., opposite_distance=)` stores a `two_sides` extent and checks its extent arguments in the order of python-api.md 3.5; `chamfer(..., second_distance=, flip=)` stores `two_distances` with `flip` only when true; `linear_pattern(..., reverse=, second_reverse=)` stores `reverse` in each direction only when true, and `_direction` takes the flag. The hooks of `crates/partsmith-features/src/builtin/extrude.rs`, `chamfer.rs` and `linear_pattern.rs` write the keywords in signature order, so chamfer's hook always gives a dedicated form and the other two give `None` only when a variable is missing. The Python bless rewrote the 8 twins (`chamfer/edge-order`, `two-distances`, `two-distances-flipped`; `extrude/two-sides`, `two-sides-one-zero`, `two-sides-reverse`; `linear_pattern/reverse`, `second-reverse`), and only `hole/deleted-point` keeps a generic call. Pass: python-api.md tests 82 and 107 to 113, delivery.md test 72, the hook unit tests and features.md test 23. The web core was rebuilt from this tree. Reverting is option (C) of the ADR.
- **Status (2026-10-28, review fixes):** done, no behavior defect found; still waiting for the human gate of ADR 0048 decision 1. Test 113 now takes its corpus from `support.py` (`example_models`, `twinned_models`, `twin_of`, `example_id` and `TWINNED`), shared with `test_examples.py` and test 91 of `test_references.py`: an example is a `.ptsm` with its `.py` beside it, outside the `assets` folders below `examples/`, so a checkout under a folder named `assets` still checks the corpus and a stray `.py` is no example (three tests in `test_examples.py`). The extrude hook's unit tests share one `rectangle_params` helper. `approximately`, `near` and `stored_params` moved into `support.py` from the four test modules that copied them.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0048 decision 1 (options (B) to (D), reverting being option (C)) is open.

Row 5 is measured too, since the plan's PY2 is merged. **SK-2. No nightly job runs the property tests at 4096 cases**
- **Spec:** sketch.md 6.13; crates/partsmith-sketch/AGENTS.md.
- **Spec first:** yes. delivery.md 1.1 plus a section 6 test, or a 1.2 deferral.
- **Work:** a scheduled workflow (cron and `workflow_dispatch`) that runs `PROPTEST_CASES=4096 cargo test -p partsmith-sketch --release --test integration properties::`. It needs no OCCT cache. List it in `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md`.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** B-1.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** workflow written, on `main`. `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` follows delivery.md 1.1 and 2.11 (cron `23 4 * * *` and `workflow_dispatch`; a scheduled run skips when the newest concluded nightly run already tested the head of `main`; `timeout-minutes: 20`, now in 2.11, caps a run that never finishes), with the skip notice of section 4, the budget of 5 and section 8; sketch.md 6.13 points at it, and `.github/workflows/AGENTS.md` lists it. Changed in review: the skip check counted only runs that passed or failed, so a run stopped by `timeout-minutes`, which does not conclude `failure`, was repeated every night on an unchanged `main`, and a failing `gh` failed the step, recording a run that later nights counted as tested. It now asks for the newest finished run (`gh run list --status completed --limit 1`), whatever its conclusion, and on a `gh` failure warns and tests (2.11, the warning of section 4). Test 51 in `ui/scripts/workflows.test.mjs` runs the step's script with `bash -e` and a stub `gh` (the head tested, another commit, no finished run, `gh` failing); the real `gh` 2.89.0 accepts the same arguments on this repository's `ci.yml` runs, and the 4096-case run passes (15 tests in 3.5 s). Test 48 needs the workflow on `main`: one `workflow_dispatch` run, then a scheduled night with `main` unchanged and one after a new commit. Measured on a 4-core machine: a cold release build of the sketch tests takes 2 min 26 s and the 4096-case run 1.6 s, so a night costs about 3 billed minutes after a change and 1 when skipped.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** the nightly has run every night since 2026-10-17 (11 scheduled runs, no `workflow_dispatch` run yet). Nights after a new commit on `main` ran the tests and passed until 2026-10-27, and nights with `main` unchanged ended within 11 s, the length of a skip (2026-10-20 to 22 and 2026-10-25 to 26). Run 40484421689 (2026-10-27, 5153d61, which changed no sketch code) failed in sketch.md test 192 (translation), as its log shows (read through the GitHub connector): after 3,045 passing cases, seed 4349971845757929081 with offset (0, -188.8237558036373) gives the circle `e-1` radius 13.42024442090656 where the original solve gives 13.428402502933308. Run here on the tree of cae269a, the 4096 cases fail now and then in two tests, on a different case each time. Test 192: a moved warm start can solve to a different result, once in 11 runs (seed 1152530511282187205, offset (0, 105.07068264498119): the arc `e-1`'s radius 9.1e-5 off against the test's 1e-6) and once in a review's 102 (seed 15957013116401757264, offset (0, 465.96742262759614)). sketch.md test 194 (drag frame): the frame's solve does not converge (its assertion `the solve converged` at `properties.rs:271` fails), once in that review's 102 runs (seed 17871216647167640405, choice 822554227111491894, offset (0.06655742848476154, 0)) and twice in 160 more, in which test 192 never failed (seed 17307699197065700453, choice 5947579853394604854, offset (0.0301811009152872, 0); seed 12268459693006179818, choice 6275455837359716385, offset (0, -0.06627064037391135)). Running the nightly's command locally appends each failing case to the tracked `crates/partsmith-sketch/tests/properties.proptest-regressions`; such a line belongs only in the change that fixes its test. Test 48 still needs its `workflow_dispatch` run, and tests 192 and 194 a fix in partsmith-sketch.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** engineering work is left besides the human gate. The nightly has run every night since, all scheduled: it tested fba52b0 on 2026-10-28 and 0a976b4 on 2026-10-30 and passed, and skipped on 2026-10-29 with `main` unchanged; test 48 still needs a `workflow_dispatch` run, which takes no code. Tests 192 and 194 still fail now and then: the crate's source and `tests/properties.rs` have not changed since 2026-10-23 (its later commits, 9640b91 and 03d6265, changed other tests, test helpers and its AGENTS.md), and 80 runs here of the nightly's command on this tree failed 4 times, test 192 twice (`properties.rs:221`, the translated result more than 1e-6 off) and test 194 twice (`properties.rs:271`, the drag frame's checks), each on another random case; a review's 260 runs failed 6 times, test 192 four times and test 194 twice. They need a fix in partsmith-sketch, which commits the `cc` line of each case it fixes (delivery.md 2.11); these runs left `tests/properties.proptest-regressions` unchanged. The human gate is the nightly workflow of delivery.md 8 (its schedule, runner time and skip rule).
- **Status (2026-10-31, spec):** root causes found, both in the solver; the fix is specified, no code yet (sketch.md 3.7.4, 3.9 step 5, tests 256 and 257, ADR 0052). Replayed from their seeds in a scratch copy of the crate, the six recorded cases fail as recorded. 200 runs of the nightly command on this tree failed 8 times, 4 in each test. With two from a first batch, that gives 10 new cases, each with its `cc` line. Test 192: every case has a constraint that repeats others on the solution set (an `angle` that follows from a `collinear` and another `angle`, a point-line distance beside the same distance back between parallel lines, a `horizontal_distance` equal to a length), whose row the second Gauss-Newton step accepted with a part of 1.2e-8 to 7.7e-8 outside the span, just above `RANK_TOLERANCE`. The step through it is 1e4 to 1e6 mm long. Dog-leg follows it to its trust radius along a free direction. The rounding it leaves in later rows, of the threshold's size, decides whether the next row is accepted. This is how a translation changes the slide (1e-6 to 0.04 mm). The same steps slide geometry without a translation: 7 of 97,574 generated cases moved a coordinate 0.39 to 6.6 mm, against sketch.md 3.11. Test 194: every frame drags a point past an arc's reach (an arc with a fixed end and a set radius whose other end goes past a diameter, or an arc's start held at a distance from a fixed point while its center moves away). Near the solution only the dragged parameters can help, and the drag weight makes them 10^4 times costlier. Dog-leg and Levenberg-Marquardt creep and stop after 100 iterations each, while the same frame without the weight converges in two. Neither generator nor property asks for more than sketch.md promises (3.9, 3.11). Tests 192 and 194 and their generator are unchanged. A prototype of both changes (`STEP_RANK_TOLERANCE = 1e-6` in `numeric.rs`, a weight argument to `solve_component` and the second solve in `drag.rs`) passes every case, the 471 integration tests unchanged and 420 runs of the nightly command without a failure (one release build, run after run on this 4-core machine), with the solver benchmarks of sketch.md 5 at their medians (chain drag frame 1.31 ms against 1.32 to 1.55 ms before). The 10 new cases' `cc` lines were kept in no file. The five cases recorded in local runs on 2026-10-27 have none. Changed by the tests (2026-10-31): `tests/properties.proptest-regressions` gets 20 `cc` lines, the one the log of run 40484421689 printed for its case of test 192 (`cc 13ae955885c31d5c41ee7f4c2fd215b533069b8088f68ff9f407e2efa8c8ba03`, seed 4349971845757929081) and the 19 cases (7 of test 192, 12 of test 194) of the 18 runs that failed in 300 runs of the nightly command on this tree, each failing here and passing with a scratch prototype of ADR 0052; the six recorded cases are replayed from their seeds by `t192_recorded_cases_translate` and `t194_recorded_frames_converge` on every target (sketch.md 6.13); tests 256 and 257 and the unit tests of the step tolerance (`src/solver/numeric.rs`, red until the change) and of the diagnosis's tolerance and of test 257's weighted solve (`src/solver/diagnosis.rs`, `src/solver/drag.rs`, green as guards) are written. With the prototype, the crate's 190 unit and 476 integration tests pass. The unit test of `drag.rs` calls `solve_component` as it is today; a weight argument (the prototype's) makes it pass `DRAG_WEIGHT`. The implementation runs the nightly command (at least 400 runs), `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch` natively and with `--target wasm32-unknown-emscripten` (after `source /opt/emsdk/emsdk_env.sh`, as CI's `kernel-wasm` runs it), the benchmarks, the examples and `web_parity`, fixes the stale sentence of `SparseQr::add`'s doc comment in its cleanup pass (sketch.md 3.8 and 3.7.4 state the floor of 1), and writes the status line that drops SK-2 from the engineering exceptions of "Status at the end of M1" and adds it to the items that wait only for a human (the nightly workflow of delivery.md 8; test 48 its `workflow_dispatch` run). Only CI checks Windows and macOS: the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14) is the first to compare engine results built on the changed solver there.
- **Status (2026-10-31):** ADR 0052 implemented in partsmith-sketch; engineering work is left besides the human gate. The nightly command still fails about once in a thousand runs on a third cause (sketch.md open issue 23). Code: `src/solver/numeric.rs` ranks the rows of every Gauss-Newton step (dog-leg's and the refinement's) at the private `STEP_RANK_TOLERANCE` (1e-6) while the diagnosis keeps `RANK_TOLERANCE`; `solve_component` in `src/solver/mod.rs` takes the drag weight (1 outside a drag), and `NativeDragSession::update` in `src/solver/drag.rs` solves a frame that fails with `DRAG_WEIGHT` once more with 1 (sketch.md 3.9 step 5); the unit test of `drag.rs` passes `DRAG_WEIGHT`, no other test changed. Cleanup: `SparseQr::add`'s doc comment no longer says sketch.md lacks the floor of 1, `RANK_TOLERANCE`'s doc comment reads as in sketch.md 2.1, and the crate AGENTS.md names both changes. Root causes checked first on the unchanged solver: the six recorded cases and tests 256 and 257 failed as recorded; in the case of seed 15957013116401757264 the second step accepted the repeating row with a part of 2.08e-8, and in the translated solve with 1.43e-8, through steps of 2.4e5 and 3.5e5 mm, and `e-1`'s free end ended 0.04 mm apart (with the change neither solve accepts it, the end moves about 1e-3 from the warm start and the two results agree within 1e-12); the frame of seed 17871216647167640405 drags the end of an arc with a fixed start, a set diameter and a sweep of 179.5°, and dog-leg stopped 2.4e-2 off. With the change: `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch` passes its 190 unit and 476 integration tests natively and under Node (`--target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`), test 255's literals unchanged; the tests of the crates that depend on it pass (partsmith-features, partsmith-protocol, partsmith-recompute, partsmith-engine, partsmith-cli, partsmith-wasm, partsmith-py and partsmith-desktop), and fmt and clippy with `-D warnings` are clean; the examples command passes with no expected file moved: nothing is blessed and kernel.md open issue 14 needs no check; the web core rebuilt by `scripts/build-wasm.sh` passes `web_module` and `web_parity` (engine.md test 154). The solver benchmarks of sketch.md 5, three runs each of the old and the new solver on this 4-core machine: the chain drag frame's median 1.38 to 1.53 ms against 1.50 to 1.70 ms, every frame at most 4.8 ms, `begin_drag` 3.53 to 3.67 ms against 3.55 to 3.64 ms, the solve with every radius 6 4.49 to 4.55 ms against 4.53 to 4.60 ms, the satisfied chain 2.92 to 3.00 ms against 2.94 to 3.02 ms, the plate 0.028 to 0.029 ms against 0.029 ms, the profile and matching rows unchanged, all within budget. The nightly command on one release build, four runs at a time: a first batch failed in its 368th run, in test 192 (`the original solve converged`, seed 2825233946436231350, offset (0, 0)), then 600 runs in a row passed. In all, 967 of 968 runs passed. That case fails with the old solver too: a distance and a vertical distance put an arc's start 9.1e-4 mm beside the line it touches, dog-leg ends at the fold where the start touches the line and Levenberg-Marquardt stops 3.3e-9 off. A scan of the warm starts of seeds 0 to 3,999,999 (3,903,910 cases) found one more case next to a fold, seed 1947428, which converges with `RANK_TOLERANCE` and fails with the step tolerance (ADR 0052's consequences). The change covers neither. Neither `cc` line is committed: the failing run's line was taken back out of `tests/properties.proptest-regressions`, which keeps the tests' 20 lines, every one of which passes. Open issue 23 gives the options (a generator rule like the one for points closer than 0.1, a second dog-leg with `RANK_TOLERANCE` before Levenberg-Marquardt, or a step that keeps a near-dependent row while the step through it stays short); one of them, with its spec text and tests, comes before SK-2 waits only for the human gate (the nightly workflow of delivery.md 8; test 48 needs only its `workflow_dispatch` run, which the orchestrator starts after the merge). Only CI checks Windows and macOS: the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14) is the first to compare engine results built on the changed solver there.
- **Status (2026-10-31, review fixes):** the recorded causes of tests 192 and 194 are fixed and SK-2 waits only for its human gate, the nightly workflow of delivery.md 8 (its schedule, runner time and skip rule); test 48 needs only a `workflow_dispatch` run, which the orchestrator starts after the merge. A review of the line above found three defects, each checked here before a change. (1) Seed 2825233946436231350 fails from its warm start with the solver of 6901359 and with ADR 0052 alike: its distance and vertical distance leave the arc's start 9.1e-4 beside the line it touches, a tenth of the warm start's noise, and both methods stop on that fold. Sketch.md promises a solution from a warm start within reach, not across a fold. Now sketch.md 6.13 discards a distance beside an axis distance on the same two points whose separation across the other axis is above 1e-9 and below 0.1, and three distances whose triangle is that high (90 of 975,918 cases; a separation or height of zero up to rounding is a shape other constraints keep and stays); `generator_discards_solutions_next_to_a_fold` in `tests/properties.rs` checks this seed and 1947428 on every target. The failing run's `cc` line was never committed and cannot be restored. With the case discarded it would replay nothing. (2) Seed 1947428's warm start converged on 6901359 and failed with the step tolerance alone, a nearly flat triangle a user could draw: sketch.md 3.7.4 and ADR 0052 decision 3 add a second dog-leg pass with `RANK_TOLERANCE` before Levenberg-Marquardt, which together are the solve as it ran before. No solve that converged then fails now, and 1947428 solves bitwise to its old result. Test 258 in `tests/solve.rs` (red with the step tolerance alone) and three unit tests in `src/solver/numeric.rs` (the first pass and Levenberg-Marquardt stop short there, the second pass converges) cover it. (3) A drag frame in a conflicting component ran both solves of step 5 for nothing: `NativeDragSession::begin` records whether the dragged component failed with a conflict and `update` skips step 5 for it (sketch.md 3.9, ADR 0052 decision 2), with three unit tests in `src/solver/drag.rs`. The chain of sketch.md 5.2 with `radius e-24-ar = 6`, dragged by (-1, 1) a frame, takes 99 to 116 ms a frame against 166 to 199 ms before and 84 to 96 ms on 6901359 (the rest is the second dog-leg pass). `numeric::solve` loops over the two tolerances: a first form with two dog-leg calls slowed the chain drag frame from about 1.4 ms to 1.6 to 1.8 ms in a scratch probe although every one of its 1,000 frames converges in the first pass. Cleanup: `point_entity` and `points_distance` in `src/test_support.rs` serve the new unit tests and the fixtures that built them inline, `with_problem` the unit tests of `numeric.rs`, and the fold rule's helpers use descriptive names. Checks: `cargo test -p partsmith-sketch` passes its 196 unit and 478 integration tests natively and under Node (`--target wasm32-unknown-emscripten`), test 255's literals unchanged; the tests of partsmith-features, partsmith-protocol, partsmith-recompute, partsmith-engine, partsmith-cli, partsmith-wasm, partsmith-py and partsmith-desktop pass; fmt and clippy with `-D warnings` are clean; the examples command passes its 118 tests with no expected file moved: nothing is blessed and kernel.md open issue 14 needs no check; the web core rebuilt by `scripts/build-wasm.sh` passes `web_module` and `web_parity` (engine.md test 154). The nightly command on one release build, run after run: 1,000 runs passed on the tree before the loop form (the same kept seeds and results), and of 820 runs on the final build one failed, in test 192 (seed 5635218625053008121, offset (0, 487.4378466513926), 1.2e-6 off): a 5.36 mm slide of sketch.md open issue 21 that fails the same way on 6901359. Its `cc` line was taken back out because nothing fixes it. A scan of the warm starts of seeds 0 to 19,999,999 with the checks of tests 189, 192 and 194 found two failures besides: seed 1947428's drag frame, which fails on 6901359 too and is now discarded, and seed 9841460, a slide of the same kind as 5635218625053008121 (open issue 21). `tests/properties.proptest-regressions` keeps its 24 lines, each still generated and passing. The Playwright suite passes on Chromium (229 tests) with the rebuilt web core. The benchmarks of sketch.md 5, alternating rounds of workspace builds of 6901359, the implementation above and this tree on this 4-core machine: the chain drag frame's median 1.51 to 1.75, 1.37 to 1.61 and 1.55 to 1.80 ms, `begin_drag` 3.53 to 4.50, 3.57 to 3.68 and 3.54 to 4.48 ms, the solve with every radius 6 4.45 to 4.84, 4.45 to 4.65 and 4.52 to 5.36 ms, the satisfied chain 2.89 to 3.02, 2.91 to 3.03 and 2.92 to 3.14 ms, the plate 0.027 to 0.032 ms for each, all within budget. Every chain frame converges in the first pass (1,000 of 1,000). A frame does the work it did before. A scratch probe timing the same 1,000 frames with the workspace's thin LTO gives 1.40 to 1.50 ms for this tree against 1.39 to 1.54 ms for the implementation above and 1.56 to 1.64 ms for 6901359. The spread follows code layout and the machine's load. The line above's 1.50 to 1.70 ms for 6901359 lies within it and shows no speedup. Only CI checks Windows and macOS: the next platforms run (delivery.md 2.14) is the first to compare engine results built on the changed solver there.

### partsmith-cli

**CLI-2. The CLI budgets of cli.md 5 are not measured**
- **Spec:** cli.md 5.
- **Spec first:** small. A line on how they are measured, plus an ADR 0027 list entry or a new ADR.
- **Human gate:** dependencies (ADR 0027 amendment of 2026-10-30, proposed, needs human review).
- **Work:**
  - `benches/budgets.rs` on partsmith-budgets, timing the release binary through `std::process::Command`.
  - A 200 KB workload, generated at run time or added as an `examples/bench/<name>`.
  - rebuild and export on fifty-features, at the engine budget plus 100 ms.
  - Cargo.toml bench wiring, and the command in the crate's AGENTS.md.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** M.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-30, spec):** spec text written, no code yet (ADR 0027, amendment of 2026-10-30). cli.md section 5 now says how its rows are measured: `cargo bench -p partsmith-cli --bench budgets` on `partsmith-budgets` runs the binary Cargo builds for benchmarks (the release profile) through `std::process::Command` in a temporary directory, one untimed run and then the median of 11 per row, each run required to exit 0 (a row whose run does not is reported not measured, and the other rows are still measured); six named rows, with `rebuild` and `export` of the reference part at 1.1 s and 1.4 s (the engine's cold `open_document`, 1 s, and its binary STL `export`, 300 ms, plus 100 ms); and the 200 KB document written at run time, `plate.ptsm` 133 times over (200,745 bytes), from `tests/common/workload.rs`, which the bench includes alone with `#[path]` and test 64 checks on every test run. No `examples/bench` document is added. Work that comes with the code: `[[bench]]` and the `partsmith-budgets` dev-dependency in `crates/partsmith-cli/Cargo.toml`, `tests/common/workload.rs` (declared `pub mod workload;` in `tests/common/mod.rs`), test 64 in `crates/partsmith-cli/tests/budgets.rs` (declared in `tests/integration.rs`, run with `cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration budgets::`), the bench command, `tests/common/workload.rs` and `tests/budgets.rs` in `crates/partsmith-cli/AGENTS.md`, and the bench's first medians in cli.md open issue 11.
- **Status (2026-10-30, implementation):** done. `crates/partsmith-cli/benches/budgets.rs` runs the six rows of cli.md section 5 (`cargo bench -p partsmith-cli --bench budgets`, words after `--` select rows), `tests/common/workload.rs` writes the 200 KB document with `budget_document()`, and test 64 passes (`cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration budgets::`, 8 tests). Two runs here gave 5.7 to 20.9 ms for the first four rows and 2,228 to 2,727 ms for `rebuild` and `export` of the reference part, over their 1.1 s and 1.4 s as the engine's cold `open_document` is over here (cli.md open issue 11); a run that does not exit 0 was checked to leave its row not measured while the other rows are measured.
- **Status (2026-10-30, review fixes):** done. engine.md section 5 and the engine's AGENTS.md now say that the desktop budgets of the cold `open_document` and binary STL `export` rows also set cli.md section 5's `rebuild` and `export` rows; the ADR log and ADR 0027 mark the 2026-10-30 amendment proposed, needs human review; cli.md open issue 11 keeps the bench's medians and the engine comparison without the prototype's; `copy_id`'s comment says what it maps; the bench writes a failed run's command text only when a run fails. A run after the fixes gave 6.5, 22.8, 20.1 and 15.6 ms for the first four rows and 2,258 and 2,592 ms for `rebuild` and `export`, over as before; a run whose `rebuild` was killed still printed the not-measured message and measured the other selected row.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; only the human gate of ADR 0027's amendment of 2026-10-30 (proposed) is open. The `rebuild` and `export` rows over their budgets here are cli.md open issue 11, which only a run on the reference machine decides.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0027's amendment of 2026-10-30 as built. cli.md open issue 11 stays as it is.

### partsmith-protocol

**PROTO-1. The protocol AGENTS.md says `Edit` accepts unknown fields**
- **Spec:** model.md 2.9 (line 568); protocol.md 2.3.
- **Spec first:** no.
- **Work:**
  - Fix line 24 of `crates/partsmith-protocol/AGENTS.md`: `Edit` rejects unknown fields, while the `FeatureRecord` inside it keeps unknown keys as params.
  - Add an acceptance test that `apply_edits` rejects an unknown `Edit` field, if none exists.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** nothing.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** done. `Edit` already rejected unknown fields (`deny_unknown_fields`, model.md 2.9, with unit tests in `crates/partsmith-model/src/edit.rs`), but no protocol test covered `apply_edits`. protocol.md test 85 (`tests/frame_decoding.rs`, the two `t085_` tests) now does: an unknown field in an `update_parameter` or a `set_rollback` edit is `InvalidMessage` with the request id (`malformed_request`), while the `FeatureRecord` of an `add_feature` keeps its unknown key as a param. With `deny_unknown_fields` taken off `Edit` on purpose, the first test fails. The protocol AGENTS.md says `Edit` rejects unknown fields.

### Delivery and licensing (outside the blockers)

**DEL-1. The CC0 grant has no license text** (resolved: ADR 0028 puts everything under Apache-2.0)
- **Spec:** technical design 3.
- **Spec first:** an ADR that records the grant and its scope.
- **Human gate:** license.
- **Work:**
  - A root `LICENSE-CC0`. It cannot go under `examples/`, because 2.8 forbids it there.
  - A README list of the exact paths: `docs/spec/file-format.md`, `docs/format/ptsm.schema.json`, `examples/**`.
  - Optional: an SPDX note in file-format.md.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** the same license gate as B-2.
- **Status (2026-10-27):** resolved by ADR 0028 (accepted by the owner on 2026-10-13), so the work above does not apply: no `LICENSE-CC0` and no CC0 scope. No document promises a CC0 grant: README.md (License), the technical design (section 3), delivery.md 2.12 part 7 and ADR 0028 put the code, specifications, file format, examples and test corpus under Apache-2.0, and every package manifest says `Apache-2.0`. CC0 appears elsewhere only as a license `deny.toml` allows for dependencies such as `blake3`, `constant_time_eq` and `dunce` (in the dependency logs, ADR 0033, the notices, delivery.md 2.10 and its fixture in `ui/scripts/check-licenses.test.mjs`), and in ADR 0028's account of the grant it replaced. Technical design 17 said that cargo-deny allows "the listed LGPL exceptions (OCCT, PlaneGCS)", "protecting the MIT/Apache license"; as ADR 0034 asks, it now says that cargo-deny covers crates and protects the Apache-2.0 license, while OCCT, not a crate, is covered by the notices and published source of delivery.md 2.9 and 2.13, and section 3's license row says that OCCT is linked statically instead of calling OCCT and PlaneGCS separate libraries. No human gate is left.

**DEL-2. No CI check covers npm licenses**
- **Spec:** technical design 17; the AGENTS.md license invariant.
- **Spec first:** yes. delivery.md 1.1, 2.4 and 6, using the deny.toml SPDX allow list.
- **Human gate:** dependencies and licenses.
- **Work:**
  - A `ui/scripts` node script over `pnpm licenses list --prod --json`, with a `node:test` test and a `ui/package.json` script.
  - A step in the `ui` job.
  - An ADR entry that logs the npm packages and their licenses.
- **Impact:** low. **Size:** S.
- **Depends on:** B-1. Share the license data with B-2.
- **Status (2026-10-16):** code written with B-2, which needs its exports, on `main`, where the `ui` job runs `pnpm check:licenses` on every push, human gate open. `ui/scripts/check-licenses.mjs` with `pnpm check:licenses` (tests 43 to 47 pass; it reports 11 npm packages that ship, each under a license `deny.toml` allows) and its step in the `ui` job. It also exports `compareText`, `compareNameAndVersion`, `runCommand` and `commandFailureMessage`, which the notices generator shares (delivery.md 2.10). ADR 0033 logs the eleven packages: eight production packages (seven MIT, one ISC) and `rolldown`, `tailwindcss` and `vite` (all MIT), whose code the build writes into `ui/dist`. The other development dependencies hold MIT-0, Python-2.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0; they do not ship, and the check leaves them out. Rechecked by the DEL-2 implementer: 84 script tests pass (90 with the workflow tests 49 to 51 and the new cases of test 38), the command takes about 2 s (budget 10 s), and on a copy of the script with a narrowed `deny.toml` it exits 1 with one not-allowed message per package, the deny.toml message, or the `pnpm` failure message.
- **Status (2026-10-30):** no engineering work is left; `pnpm check:licenses` has passed in CI's `ui` job on every push to `main` (the latest in run 41103065188, 228ad1d), and only the human gate is open: ADR 0033 (proposed), which logs the npm packages that ship, and the npm license policy of delivery.md 8.
- **Status (2026-11-04, decisions):** done, and signed off: the owner accepted ADR 0033 as built, with the npm packages that ship and the npm license policy of delivery.md 8.
- **Status (2026-11-05, decisions):** the license check reaches the dependency archives. `licenseAllowed` runs over every npm package they hold and fails the archive step on a license outside the policy. `deny.toml` allows MIT-0, Python-2.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0 with an ADR row. The owner signed this license-policy change on 2026-11-05 (delivery.md 8). Wave G's archive-contents track builds it.
- **Status (2026-11-05, archive-contents spec):** specified (delivery.md 2.10 and 2.13, test 108, ADR 0033's amendment of 2026-11-05). `scripts/dependency-source-archive.mjs` reads the `license` of the `package.json` in each npm tarball it archives and fails on a license `deny.toml` does not allow. The list gains MIT-0, Python-2.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0. All 160 packages of the desktop scope pass. `cargo deny check licenses` still passes. `check:licenses`'s code is unchanged; its tests 43, 44 and 46 follow the list.
- **Status (2026-11-05, archive-contents fixer):** built and reviewed. The license check reads a `package.json` under a ustar prefix, a pax `path` or a GNU long name (delivery.md test 108). Every npm package of both scopes passes it.

## Suggested sequence

1. **Now, alongside the phases:**
   - Human: B-1 setup; the B-2 license gate.
   - Plan edits: R-1, R-2.
   - Small items: CLI-1, UI-1, PROTO-1, B-12.
   - Draft B-13.
2. **Wave A, right after the phases (data safety and hosts):**
   - B-3 and B-4 together, then B-5 (with PY-1's spec) and B-6 sharing one desktop capability review, then B-7.
   - In parallel: B-8, B-9, B-10, B-11.
3. **Wave B (M1 behavior and corpus):** PRINT-1; EX-1 to EX-5; ENG-1, ENG-4 and SK-1 as one wasm CI job; ENG-2.
4. **Wave C (hardening):** EX-6 to EX-10, IMP-1, PRINT-2, PRINT-3, ENG-3, ENG-5, ENG-6, UI-2 to UI-5, PY-2, PY-3, CLI-2, SK-2, DEL-2.

Before the owner's decisions of 2026-11-04, these items waited for a human: B-1; B-2; B-5; B-8 (ADR 0038 and ADR 0039, recorded in delivery.md 8, and the 30 degree angle of ADR 0041, recorded in print.md 8); B-9 (ADR 0038, recorded in delivery.md 8); B-10 (Python 3.13 on CI, recorded in delivery.md 8); B-13 (the web app's address and the AppImage choice); EX-6 (only whether runner test 5 also imports each STEP file back, delivery.md open issue 18, asked in delivery.md 8); PRINT-1 (only the Python API of ADR 0040); PRINT-2; PRINT-3; PY-2; UI-2 (the security review of ADR 0049 decision 1 and the checks of delivery.md open issues 17 and 19); UI-3 (the scope of the target size rule, ADR 0049 decision 3, as the ADR log records it, and the macOS tab order check of ui.md open issue 43); IMP-1 (ADR 0045); CLI-2 (the 2026-10-30 amendment of ADR 0027: partsmith-cli's budget benchmark and its partsmith-budgets dev-dependency); SK-2 (the nightly workflow, recorded in delivery.md 8); ENG-8 (ADR 0053); and DEL-2. B-6 and DEL-1 had left the list before then: ADR 0037 decision 1 needs no review, and ADR 0028 resolved DEL-1. What still waits for the owner after the decisions of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05 is in the paragraph near the top that starts "What stays with the owner after wave G".
